### DL-074 — Common settings requests lacked deterministic deep-links

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #settings #agent-recipes #intent-dispatch #policy` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug |
| **File(s)** | `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/action/AssistantAction.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/llm/AvailableTools.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/llm/AvailableToolsTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/intent/AndroidIntentDispatcher.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/AndroidSettingsRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngine.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/AndroidSettingsRecipeTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | Requests for ringtone, sound, Do Not Disturb, brightness, or screen-timeout settings had no deterministic Android Settings target, so the settings recipe refused them and the action tool schema did not advertise those targets. |
| **Root Cause** | `SettingsTarget` only covered the original first recipe pack. New common Settings screens were never appended to the serialized enum, dispatcher, recipe matcher, policy allowlist, or tool schema. During validation, the requested `Settings.ACTION_ZEN_MODE_SETTINGS` branch also failed to compile because android-36 exposes the `android.settings.ZEN_MODE_SETTINGS` activity action but not a public `Settings` field for it. |
| **Fix** | Appended `RINGTONE`, `DND`, `BRIGHTNESS`, and `SCREEN_TIMEOUT` with stable serial names; mapped them to Sound, Zen Mode, and Display settings intents; added keyword resolution; explicitly allowed them in `isTooSensitiveForSettingsRecipe`; and synced the `dispatch_action` schema. DND uses a local action-string constant for `android.settings.ZEN_MODE_SETTINGS` because the SDK jar does not expose a public field. |
| **Validation** | Plain `./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug` first failed before Gradle because no Java runtime was on PATH. With the repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug` passed; Gradle reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 27s` and `179 actionable tasks: 61 executed, 118 up-to-date`. `git diff --check` passed. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When adding a new `SettingsTarget`, append the enum value, update the dispatcher, recipe matcher, policy allow/deny arm, and advertised `dispatch_action` schema in the same change; verify any Android Settings action against the compiled SDK jar because some activity actions exist without public `Settings.ACTION_*` constants. |

### DL-075 — AvailableToolsTest used unavailable JsonPrimitive.content import

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#core #tests #serialization #build` |
| **Severity** | Build Error |
| **File(s)** | `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/llm/AvailableToolsTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | After changing the dispatch-action schema assertion to parse JSON, `:core:compileTestKotlin` failed with `Unresolved reference 'content'`. |
| **Root Cause** | This repo's kotlinx.serialization version exposes and already uses `JsonPrimitive.contentOrNull`, but the new test imported `kotlinx.serialization.json.content`, which is not available on the configured dependency version. |
| **Fix** | Replaced the unavailable `content` import/property with `contentOrNull` and `mapNotNull`, matching the existing serialization idiom in the codebase. |
| **Validation** | `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug` passed; Gradle reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1s` and `179 actionable tasks: 3 executed, 176 up-to-date`. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When parsing kotlinx.serialization JSON in tests, use helpers already present in the repo (`contentOrNull`, `jsonObject`, `jsonArray`) instead of assuming newer extension properties are available. |

### DL-076 — InstallAppRecipe opens Play Store without auto-installing

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #agent-recipes #install-app #intent-dispatch #policy` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/action/AssistantAction.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/llm/AvailableTools.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/InstallAppRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/ClockRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/intent/AndroidIntentDispatcher.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngine.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/llm/HandyToolRunner.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/InstallAppRecipeTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/androidTest/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/intent/AndroidIntentDispatcherInstallAppTest.kt`, `docs/ACTION_POLICY.md`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The canonical `install_app` intent existed in the router but had no runtime recipe or dispatch action, so requests such as "install Spotify" could not deterministically open a visible Play Store handoff. Play Store URLs with payment-like package or query tokens could also be falsely blocked by the generic payment URL beta guard. |
| **Root Cause** | The recipe router was prepared for the install-app family in DL-072, but the serialized `AssistantAction`, Android dispatcher branch, runtime recipe, prompt example, schema, and policy confirmation behavior had not landed together. The payment URL guard matched decoded URL tokens without recognizing Play Store listing/search URLs as a safe handoff where the user still controls Install and purchases. |
| **Fix** | Added `AssistantAction.InstallApp(packageHint, searchQuery)` with invariant validation, `InstallAppRecipe`, runtime recipe registration, prompt/schema coverage, and dispatcher support for `market://details` / `market://search` with HTTPS Play Store chooser fallback on `ActivityNotFoundException`. Policy now allows InstallApp as `MEDIUM` risk with `NORMAL` confirmation, includes package hints in denylist targeting, and exempts Play Store listing/search URLs from the payment-word URL block. |
| **Validation** | Focused checks passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.PromptCatalogTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.llm.AvailableToolsTest' :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.InstallAppRecipeTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.action.DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.RuntimeRecipePackTest' --stacktrace`. Dispatcher fallback instrumentation smoke passed on `emulator-5554` with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:/Users/satvik.bansal/Library/Android/sdk/platform-tools:$PATH ./gradlew :android-runtime:connectedDebugAndroidTest -Pandroid.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments.class=com.handy.runtime.intent.AndroidIntentDispatcherInstallAppTest --stacktrace`. Full requested validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`; Gradle reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 22s` and `179 actionable tasks: 61 executed, 118 up-to-date`. `git diff --check` passed. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When adding a new canonical recipe action, land the serialized action, dispatcher branch, policy confirmation/risk behavior, prompt example, tool schema, runtime recipe registration, docs row, and focused tests in the same change; for safe platform handoff URLs, whitelist the exact destination before applying broad payment/purchase token blocks. |

### DL-077 — Timer requests lacked recipe plan approval

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #agent-recipes #timer #intent-dispatch` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/TimerRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/ClockRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/TimerRecipeTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/RuntimeRecipePackTest.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalogTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The canonical `set_timer` intent and `AssistantAction.StartTimer` dispatch action existed, but timer requests such as "set a 10-minute timer" had no runtime recipe, so they could not be proposed through the plan-approval flow. |
| **Root Cause** | Timer support was implemented as a direct native action and router mapping, while the runtime recipe pack only wrapped the alarm side of Clock. The prompt also did not teach the agent recipe flow to emit `set_timer` with a concrete `seconds` arg. |
| **Fix** | Added `TimerRecipe` with one native `StartTimer` step, inline whole-number duration parsing, range refusal (`timer-out-of-range`) and missing-duration refusal (`missing-duration`). Registered it in `AndroidRuntimeRecipes.defaultRecipes()`, added prompt coverage for `set_timer` args, and added recipe/pack/prompt tests. During validation, the first compile pass exposed a top-level initializer-order bug in the parser regex; moving the generated number-word map above the regex fixed it before the final pass. |
| **Validation** | `git diff --check` passed. Plain `java -version` still fails on this machine with no Java runtime on PATH, so validation used the repo-local JDK. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug` passed; Gradle reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 28s` and `179 actionable tasks: 63 executed, 116 up-to-date`. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When a `RecipeIntentRouter` mapping points at a runtime recipe id, the same change must include a registered runtime recipe with that exact id plus a pack-level test proving the id is present. For top-level generated regex constants, declare all generated inputs before the regex value that interpolates them. |

### DL-078 — Web search intent lacked a native recipe handoff

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #agent-recipes #web-search #intent-dispatch #policy` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/WebSearchRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/ClockRecipe.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/agent/RecipeIntentRouter.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/intent/AndroidIntentDispatcher.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngine.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/llm/AvailableTools.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/WebSearchRecipeTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/RuntimeRecipePackTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalogTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The canonical `web_search` recipe intent existed in the router, but runtime recipes did not contain a deterministic native browser/search handoff. Requests like "google Android 16 release notes" could be confused with the `web_search` evidence tool path and consume tool quota instead of opening the user's browser/search app. |
| **Root Cause** | `AssistantAction.WebSearchIntent` and the dispatcher branch existed, but no registered runtime recipe produced that action from canonical recipe routing. The dispatcher also returned `NoHandler` when `ACTION_WEB_SEARCH` had no resolver instead of falling back to an ordinary browser URL. |
| **Fix** | Added `WebSearchRecipe` with `query` arg parsing plus text fallback for "search the web for", "search google for", "google", and "search for"; it refuses empty and over-200-character queries and emits one `NativeAction(WebSearchIntent)` step. Registered it in `AndroidRuntimeRecipes`, kept `WEB_SEARCH` routed to `web_search`, added normal-confirmation policy for browser search handoff while preserving sensitive-query blocking, added an `Intent.createChooser` fallback to `https://www.google.com/search?q=...`, and taught the prompt when to use `[INTENT:web_search]` versus the `web_search` tool. `HandyToolRunner`'s web tool/quota path was not changed. |
| **Validation** | Focused checks passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.WebSearchRecipeTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.action.DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.RuntimeRecipePackTest' --stacktrace` and `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.PromptCatalogTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.agent.RecipeIntentRouterTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.llm.AvailableToolsTest' --stacktrace`. First combined full verification hit an Android lint internal FIR-resolution crash while analyzing existing `:app` test files; isolated `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:lint --stacktrace` passed, and rerunning the exact requested command `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug` passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 4s` and `179 actionable tasks: 6 executed, 173 up-to-date`. `:app:installDebug` installed on `emulator-5554`, launching `com.handy.android/com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity` returned `Status: ok`, the process stayed alive with pid `10840`, and fresh logcat had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `am_crash`, or Handy exception/error matches. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When a canonical recipe intent has the same visible name as an LLM evidence tool, add an explicit prompt distinction, a registered runtime recipe, and a quota-preservation test path in the same change; native search/browser handoffs must include an ordinary HTTPS chooser fallback when their platform-specific intent has no resolver. |

### DL-079 — ChromeRecipe gained explicit omnibox search typing

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #agent-recipes #chrome #omnibox #tap-for-me` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/ChromeRecipe.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/agent/AgentSessionController.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/ChromeRecipeTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalogTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | Explicit requests such as "search chrome for cats" had no Chrome-specific deterministic path. Generic browser search could open a search app, and ChromeRecipe could open URLs or tap visible marks, but it could not focus Chrome's omnibox, type a query, and submit it through the visible UI. |
| **Root Cause** | `OPEN_CHROME_URL` already routed to `ChromeRecipe`, but ChromeRecipe only looked for URL or mark navigation inputs. The recipe runner also executed TypeText through the generic performer, so a Chrome omnibox step would not use the Buddy flight typing UX unless the app-side performer was narrowed for that target. |
| **Fix** | Added an explicit Chrome omnibox-search branch that accepts `query` args or text patterns like "search chrome for X", "chrome search X", and "in chrome search X" only when the foreground package is a Chromium-family package. The plan opens stable Chrome first for Brave/Edge/Vivaldi, then focuses `viewId` suffix `url_bar`, types the query, taps `Go`, and includes a native `https://www.google.com/search?q=...` fallback step. `AgentSessionController` now wraps only Chrome recipe typing into `BuddyFlightDriver.flyToAndType(SemanticPoint(viewId = "url_bar"))`; other recipe typing still uses the existing performer. Prompt guidance now keeps generic "search the web" on `[INTENT:web_search]` while routing explicit Chrome omnibox searches to `[INTENT:open_chrome_url]`. |
| **Validation** | Confirmed `BuddyFlightDriver.flyToAndType` accepts a `SemanticPoint` built from `viewId` alone. Added ChromeRecipe tests for Chrome foreground, non-Chromium refusal, Chromium-family open-Chrome prelude, and unchanged URL open; added a policy regression proving Chrome `url_bar` is not treated as a password context; added prompt coverage for explicit Chrome search versus generic web search. Focused validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.app.*' :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.ChromeRecipeTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.action.DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest' :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.PromptCatalogTest' --stacktrace`. Plain `java -version` still fails on this machine, so full validation used the repo-local JDK. The first full run hit the known intermittent Android lint FIR crash in `CoexistenceSmokeTests.kt`; rerunning the same requested task set with the repo-local JDK passed: `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 17s` and `179 actionable tasks: 18 executed, 161 up-to-date`. `git diff --check` passed. `:app:installDebug` installed on `emulator-5554`; launching `com.handy.android/com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity` returned `Status: ok`, process pid `11461` stayed alive, and fresh logcat had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `am_crash`, or Handy exception/error matches. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Chrome-specific search should remain opt-in through explicit "chrome" / "in chrome" phrasing; generic web searches must continue to route to `web_search`, and any recipe typing path that depends on Buddy motion should include a focused app-side performer test or smoke check that proves the semantic target can be constructed from stable view-id suffixes. |

### DL-080 — Summarize-screen quick prompt bypasses tools recipes and pointer flight

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #overlay-panel #quick-prompts #orchestrator #agent-recipes #tools` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/QuickPromptCatalog.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/orchestrator/ConversationOrchestrator.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/overlay/OverlayPanelState.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPanelContent.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPanelService.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayPanelBridge.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPipeline.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/orchestrator/ConversationOrchestratorTest.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/QuickPromptCatalogTest.kt`, `app/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayPresenterFsmTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The overlay quick-prompt path treated every chip as ordinary user text, so a "Summarize this screen" chip could still advertise tools, include recipe-routing prompt addendums, and let the pipeline inspect the assistant response for recipe or pointer directives. |
| **Root Cause** | Quick prompts were represented as raw strings from catalog to Compose to bridge submission. The pipeline had no typed signal that a chip requested a constrained "fast mode", so it could only run the normal tool-aware overlay turn and the normal downstream recipe/pointer post-processing. |
| **Fix** | Added typed `QuickPromptCatalog.QuickPrompt` actions with `SUMMARIZE_SCREEN` for the fallback "Summarize this screen" chip, carried that action through `OverlayPanelBridge.Submission`, mapped it to `ConversationMode.SUMMARIZE_SCREEN` in `OverlayChatPipeline`, and added the summarize prompt in `PromptCatalog`. In summarize mode the orchestrator uses the summarize prompt, strips tools to `emptyList()` regardless of the caller, avoids the tool-aware LLM path, suppresses tool-call events, and skips the first-turn intro prefix. The overlay pipeline also passes no tools for that mode and returns after displaying the response, before `AgentSessionController` recipe routing or pointer flight. |
| **Validation** | Focused checks passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.orchestrator.ConversationOrchestratorTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.QuickPromptCatalogTest' :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.app.overlay.OverlayPresenterFsmTest' --stacktrace`. Full requested validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`; Gradle reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 23s` and `179 actionable tasks: 42 executed, 137 up-to-date`. `git diff --check` passed. `:app:installDebug` installed on `emulator-5554`; launching `com.handy.android/com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity` returned `Status: ok`, process pid `12220` stayed alive, and fresh logcat had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `am_crash`, or Handy exception/error matches. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Any new "fast mode" that bypasses recipes/tools must be expressed as a `ConversationMode` value, not a string compare in the pipeline. |

|-------|-------|

| **Tags** | `#android #agent-recipes #calendar #datetime-parser #intent-dispatch` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/agent/parsing/DateTimeParser.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/agent/parsing/DateTimeParserTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/CalendarEventRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/ClockRecipe.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/CalendarEventRecipeTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent/recipes/RuntimeRecipePackTest.kt`, `android-runtime/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action/DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalogTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The canonical `create_calendar_event` intent already existed in the router and `CreateCalendarEvent` already dispatched to Calendar's insert UI, but there was no runtime recipe to safely turn requests like "schedule dentist tomorrow 3 pm" into a plan-approved native handoff. |
| **Root Cause** | Calendar support was present only as a low-level dispatch action. Without a bounded parser and registered recipe, the agent either could not propose a deterministic calendar flow or had to rely on the LLM to supply epoch milliseconds directly. |
| **Fix** | Added a pure-core `DateTimeParser` for the bounded supported phrases, with system-default timezone, past/far-future refusals, and `Result<Long?>` so callers can distinguish no time from parse failure. Added `CalendarEventRecipe` as a single `NativeAction(CreateCalendarEvent)` step that opens the OS Calendar event compose UI and never silently creates the event. Registered the recipe, kept router id parity with `create_calendar_event`, added parser-friendly prompt guidance, and covered recipe, parser, pack, prompt, and policy behavior with tests. |
| **Validation** | Focused checks passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.agent.parsing.DateTimeParserTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.PromptCatalogTest' :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.CalendarEventRecipeTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.RuntimeRecipePackTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.action.DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest' --stacktrace`. Full requested validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug`; Gradle reported `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 2s` and `179 actionable tasks: 8 executed, 1 from cache, 170 up-to-date`. `git diff --check` passed. `:app:installDebug` installed on `emulator-5554`; launching `com.handy.android/com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity` returned `Status: ok`, process pid `13129` stayed alive, and fresh logcat had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `am_crash`, or Handy exception/error matches. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Any recipe that parses natural-language time must include a table-driven parser test covering the 7 cases above and must refuse on parse error with explicit reason. |

| **Symptom** | The canonical `book_ride` intent existed but had no deterministic runtime recipe. Requests such as "book a cab to airport" could not safely prepare Uber/Ola/Rapido while preserving the product rule that the final ride confirmation remains user-initiated. |
| **Root Cause** | Runtime recipes did not have a ride-hailing pack, and the existing target selector model could not express the stable fallback shape these apps need: view-id contains matching, content-description fallback, edit-text fallback, and destination-token result matching. The action policy also did not yet block generic ride confirmation labels like Confirm, Request, or Book. |
| **Fix** | Added `RideHailingRecipePack` with Uber, Ola, and Rapido recipes capped at 4 setup steps plus an optional stable cheapest-class card step. The recipes open the app, focus/type destination, open the first matching result, optionally select a class with `STRONG_HOLD`, and never target Confirm/Request/Book/Choose/payment controls. `RecipeTarget.Node` now supports alternatives, view-id contains matching, text-token matching, and EditText/TextField role aliasing. `BOOK_RIDE` routes by app arg or foreground package, falls back to Uber, and `AgentSessionController` posts `Ready to go — tap Confirm in {app} when you're ready.` on successful ride recipe completion. Ride apps are excluded from learned gesture fallback, and policy beta-blocks Confirm/Request/Book UI targets. |
| **Validation** | `git diff --check` passed. Focused checks passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.agent.RecipeIntentRouterTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.PromptCatalogTest' :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.RideHailingRecipePackTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.agent.recipes.RuntimeRecipePackTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.action.DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest' --stacktrace`, reporting `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 6s`. Full requested validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`, reporting `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 23s` and `179 actionable tasks: 60 executed, 119 up-to-date`. `:app:installDebug` installed on `emulator-5554`; launching `com.handy.android/com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity` kept process pid `13971` alive, and fresh logcat had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `am_crash`, or Handy exception/error matches. Required real-device Uber smoke was blocked: only `emulator-5554` was attached, `pm list packages com.ubercab` returned no package, and no signed-in real Uber device was available, so no screenshot path exists. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Any recipe whose final step would be a payment / confirm / send / delete action MUST end one step before that action and post a chat message telling the user to complete the final tap in-app. |

### DL-084 — Canonical recipe requests were blocked by the old execution gate

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#core #agent-recipes #intent-routing #execution-gate` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug |
| **File(s)** | `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/agent/UserGoal.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/agent/UserGoalTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | S-series canonical recipes could be selected by the assistant but then ignored by `AgentSessionController` as guidance-only. Examples: "set a 10-minute timer", "install spotify", "google android 16 release notes", "schedule dentist tomorrow 3 pm", "book a cab to airport", "find cheap saree on meesho", "turn on dnd", "ringtone settings", "draft email to mom", and "reply to john on whatsapp". The visible failure mode would be a normal-looking assistant sentence with the internal `[INTENT:...]` stripped, but no recipe plan sheet and no action. |
| **Root Cause** | `UserGoal.allowsRecipeExecution(...)` predated the canonical recipe batch and only recognized older visible-UI verbs such as tap/open/type/search/scroll. The route smoke tests verified intent-to-recipe mapping directly, but they did not test the user-text gate that decides whether a directive is allowed to execute. |
| **Before** | Canonical S recipes routed in `RecipeIntentRouter`, but common imperative utterances like `set`, `install`, `schedule`, `book`, `find`, `draft`, and `reply` returned `false` from `allowsRecipeExecution`. |
| **After** | Expanded the executable verb pattern to cover the canonical recipe lanes and added narrowly-scoped settings noun phrases (`ringtone settings`, `screen timeout`, `brightness`, `dnd`, `do not disturb`). Guidance-only wording still wins: "how do I set a timer?", "where is brightness?", and "show me where to install spotify" remain non-executable unless the user explicitly says to do it. |
| **Validation** | Focused gate tests passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.agent.UserGoalTest' --stacktrace`. Combined focused prompt/gate tests passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test --tests 'com.handy.core.prompts.PromptCatalogTest' --tests 'com.handy.core.agent.UserGoalTest' --stacktrace`. Full local validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`, reporting `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 21s`. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When adding a canonical recipe intent, test both route selection and `UserGoal.allowsRecipeExecution(...)` for that intent's normal user-facing utterance. Route tests alone do not prove the recipe can run from the overlay pipeline. |

### DL-085 — Reduced mode still advertised direct actions and recipes

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-23 |
| **Tags** | `#android #PlayStore #reduced-mode #dispatch-action #agent-recipes` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug / Compliance Drift |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatViewModel.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPipeline.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/orchestrator/ConversationOrchestrator.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalog.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/PromptCatalogTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | `PLAYSTORE_SUBMISSION.md §4.6` and `HandySettings.accessibilityDisclosureAcknowledged` say reduced mode disables `dispatch_action` / intent firing, but full chat and overlay always passed `intentDispatchEnabled = true`. Quick overlay also always appended the agent recipe addendum and attempted recipe routing after the response, so native recipes could still be advertised or attempted while Accessibility was off. |
| **Root Cause** | The Play disclosure and onboarding state were updated independently from the LLM tool list and overlay post-processing path. The code gated screen reading and pointing on Accessibility, but it did not feed the same live state into tool availability or recipe prompting. |
| **Before** | Accessibility-off reduced mode still gave the model `dispatch_action` and quick-overlay recipe instructions; a generated directive could reach `AgentSessionController` despite the disclosure saying no Intents fire. |
| **After** | Full chat and overlay now compute `automationEnabled = accessibilityDisclosureAcknowledged && AccessibilityStateMonitor.isEnabled.value`. That value gates `availableTools(... intentDispatchEnabled = ...)`, flows through `OrchestrationRequest.agentRecipesEnabled`, suppresses the quick-overlay recipe prompt when false, and makes `OverlayChatPipeline` skip recipe routing defensively in reduced mode. |
| **Validation** | Prompt regression tests cover the reduced-mode prompt shape: quick overlay keeps `[SPOKEN]` behavior while omitting `agent-mode recipes`, `[INTENT:<canonical>]`, and `dispatch_action` when disabled. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:compileDebugKotlin --stacktrace` passed after Hilt injection changes. Full local validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`, reporting `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 21s`. Release validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleRelease --stacktrace`, reporting `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 55s`. `git diff --check` passed. Emulator crash smoke passed on `emulator-5554`: debug APK install succeeded, `am start -W -n com.handy.android/com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity` returned `Status: ok`, process pid `18618` stayed alive, and fresh `AndroidRuntime:E ActivityTaskManager:E Handy:E` logcat output was empty. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Any Play-disclosure mode must gate all three layers together: advertised tools, prompt addendums, and post-response executors. It is not enough to hide UI affordances if the LLM can still call a tool or emit a recipe directive. |

| **Root Cause** | The existing `HandyColors` palette is warm-amber `#F0A868`; the new design needs Claude-orange `#D97757` plus a multi-hue USP palette. A parallel package lets onboarding migrate without forcing a global token rewrite, and lets us A/B the new palette per-screen during the rest of the redesign rollout. |
| **Fix** | Added the parallel `com.handy.app.design` token/type/theme/primitives package, using Inter for the new typography and scene-local composition locals rather than MaterialTheme registration. Added the requested new Phosphor and Lucide vector drawables without modifying any existing drawable or `com.handy.app.theme.*` file. Documented the intentional token divergence in `DESIGN_NOTES.md`. |
| **Validation** | Confirmed `inter_regular`, `inter_medium`, `inter_semibold`, and `inter_bold` are all present in `app/src/main/res/font`. Plain `java -version` still fails on this host with "Unable to locate a Java Runtime", so Gradle validation used the repo-local JDK. `git diff --check` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed, proving the new vector pathData parses and the app compiles. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Keep redesign token systems scene-local until all dependent screens migrate. Never repoint `com.handy.app.theme.HandyColors` to a new palette just to unblock one screen family; add a parallel package with source-handoff attribution, migration scope, and validation instead. |

### DL-087 — Audit caught read-first drawable paths omitted from the onboarding asset set

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #drawables #onboarding #audit #handoff-parity` |
| **Severity** | Audit Fix / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_phosphor_mic.xml`, `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_phosphor_send.xml`, `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_lucide_camera.xml`, `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_lucide_timer.xml`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The DL-086 foundation commit added every filename from the explicit "DRAWABLES TO ADD" block, but a deep audit against the original prompt found four source paths that were called out in the earlier "FILES TO READ FIRST" block and not materialized as new source-accurate drawables: `PH_MIC`, camera, timer, and send. Existing legacy resources with similar names still existed, so no compile error surfaced, but future P-3/P-4 onboarding wiring could accidentally reuse the older stroke icons instead of the new handoff glyphs. |
| **Root Cause** | The implementation treated the later enumerated drawable filename list as exhaustive and did not reconcile it with the earlier read-first source-path list. Because the new onboarding screens were intentionally not wired yet, resource absence was latent: `assembleDebug`, unit tests, and lint could all pass while the future migration asset set was incomplete. |
| **Fix** | Added new, non-destructive drawable resources for the remaining read-first glyph sources: Phosphor mic, Phosphor paper-plane send, Lucide camera, and Lucide timer. No existing `res/drawable` file was modified, preserving the old onboarding/runtime assets until the new scenes explicitly migrate. |
| **Validation** | `git diff --check` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed, proving the additional vector pathData parses. The first `:app:test :app:lint` run hit the known intermittent Android lint FIR-resolution crash while analyzing existing test files (`CoexistenceSmokeTests.kt` / `AccessibilityGestureActionPerformerTypeTextTest.kt`); rerunning the exact same command passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 8s`. |
| **Prevention Rule** | For design-handoff asset tasks, build an explicit two-column checklist from every source-path mention and every requested output filename before editing. If the prompt contains both a read-first source list and an output-file list, reconcile them and either add the missing source-backed assets or record why each omitted source is intentionally covered by an existing resource. |

### DL-089 — ValueScreen redesigned to JSX `02a · Value (cards)`. Sheet state remains inside the screen; OnboardingActivity wires Skip to acknowledgeReducedMode + goToChat().

| Field | Value |

| **Symptom** | The Value onboarding page still rendered as a static stock-icon list instead of the handoff's `02a · Value (cards)` horizontal USP card set, and the superseded wiring assumption would have moved privacy sheet state out of its actual owner. |
| **Root Cause** | The implementation had not migrated this screen to the P-0 HandyDesign tokens and JSX scene contract. The current architecture keeps `showPrivacyDetails` inside `ValueScreen`, so the redesign needed to preserve that ownership while adding only the new Skip callback at the activity boundary. |
| **Fix** | Replaced the static list with a `HorizontalPager` plus three `USPHeroCard` composables; wired per-card amber/cobalt/emerald palettes from HandyDesign tokens; built See, Point, and Do hero scenes with Compose Canvas plus overlaid icons matching `scenes-onboarding.jsx` lines 381-530; made pager dots adopt the active card accent; kept `PrivacyDetailsBottomSheet` conditional rendering inside `ValueScreen`; and wired Skip to `viewModel.acknowledgeReducedMode()` followed by the existing no-arg `goToChat()`. Platform `Modifier.shadow` rendered a rectangular artifact through translucent card gradients on emulator, so the active card glow is drawn with scoped `drawBehind` halo layers instead. |
| **Validation** | Plain `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` is blocked on this host by the missing system Java runtime, so validation used the repo-local JDK. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`. `git diff --check` passed. Installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, captured three Value-card screenshots, visually checked the amber/blue/green cards and dots, verified the inline `What Handy sees` link opens the existing sheet, verified Skip lands in `ChatActivity`, confirmed `reduced_mode_ack` is stored as true in DataStore, and confirmed no `AndroidRuntime` / `FATAL EXCEPTION` logcat output after the smoke. Pixel 9 physical-device validation was blocked because only `emulator-5554` was attached. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When a redesign prompt mentions presenter state, first read the actual screen file to confirm whether the state already lives there before assuming a higher level owns it. |

### DL-090 — Audit pass corrected Value card hero aspect, active glow fidelity, and Skip persistence ordering.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #onboarding #visual-audit #pager #datastore #reduced-mode` |
| **Severity** | Regression Risk / Audit Fix |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/onboarding/ValueScreen.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/onboarding/OnboardingActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/onboarding/OnboardingViewModel.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | A post-implementation audit found the Value card hero scenes could read vertically stretched on taller Android safe areas, and the custom active-card glow was more banded/prominent than the JSX `box-shadow`. The new Skip path also launched chat immediately after starting the reduced-mode DataStore write, which passed emulator smoke but left a lifecycle race if the Activity/ViewModel were cleared before the write completed. |
| **Root Cause** | The first pass translated the prompt's `scaleX`/`scaleY` Canvas guidance literally and did not account for browser SVG `preserveAspectRatio` behavior in the source handoff, so 288x260 hero art was scaled to the full flexible hero slot instead of being aspect-fit inside it. The shadow workaround was validated visually after removing a worse platform-shadow artifact, but not re-audited against the original CSS blur enough. The Skip persistence risk was missed because the happy-path emulator DataStore inspection showed the flag written, while the static lifecycle ordering still allowed an avoidable race. Context compaction during the larger onboarding sequence made the pass too acceptance-check oriented: compile, screenshot, tap, then commit, instead of re-deriving the visual and lifecycle invariants from source after each workaround. |
| **Fix** | `ScaledHeroBox` now aspect-fits the 288x260 hero viewport inside the flexible card hero area, preserving source-scene proportions while keeping the card layout unchanged. The active card halo now uses smaller/lower-alpha `drawBehind` layers to approximate the CSS glow without the platform shadow artifact. `OnboardingActivity` now launches the Skip path in `lifecycleScope` and calls a new `OnboardingViewModel.acknowledgeReducedModeAndAwait()` suspend method before `goToChat()`, while the existing non-suspending `acknowledgeReducedMode()` remains for the legacy Reduced step path. |
| **Validation** | `git diff --check` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`. Installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, recaptured all three card positions, verified aspect-fit hero scenes and active/inactive states visually, verified `What Handy sees` opens the existing sheet, verified Skip lands in `ChatActivity`, confirmed `reduced_mode_ack` is stored as true (`08 01`) after the awaited path, and confirmed no `AndroidRuntime` / `FATAL EXCEPTION` logcat output. Pixel 9 physical-device validation remains blocked because only `emulator-5554` is attached. |
| **Prevention Rule** | For design-handoff Canvas ports, audit the browser rendering model as well as the numeric prompt: SVG viewBox art should be aspect-fit unless the source explicitly opts out of `preserveAspectRatio`. For navigation callbacks that persist state and immediately finish an Activity, await the persistence write or use an Activity-owned coroutine before leaving the screen. |

### DL-092 — Permissions follow-up audit anchored the CTA to the Value screen, removed redundant helper copy, and corrected the mic glyph.

It also followed the prompt's explicit `R.drawable.ic_mic` row mapping even though DL-087 had already added `ic_phosphor_mic`, so the asset audit did not reconcile latest design-system availability with the older row instruction. Context compaction during the larger onboarding sequence made the validation too checkpoint-oriented: build, tap, screenshot, and continue, rather than re-reading the latest user deltas plus prior debug-log prevention rules before accepting the visual pass. |
| **Fix** | Reworked `PermissionsStep` into a scrollable top content area plus bottom-anchored privacy/CTA band using the same CTA horizontal and bottom padding as `ValueScreen`; added a page-background fill behind the anchored band so rows cannot bleed underneath during short/landscape scroll states. Removed the accessibility helper UI and stale helper strings from the implementation path. Switched the microphone row to `R.drawable.ic_phosphor_mic`, keeping the existing permission state names, permission callbacks, notification pre-TIRAMISU skip, and no-argument `goToChat()` wiring intact. |

Physical Pixel validation remains blocked because only `emulator-5554` is attached. |
| **Prevention Rule** | For design screens with bottom CTAs, compare live UI bounds against the adjacent screen's CTA, not only against the isolated handoff numbers. After any context compaction, re-read the latest user deltas and the most relevant debug-log prevention rules before accepting a UI pass. |

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |

| **Symptom** | The Value screen's "What Handy sees" affordance still opened the older short glass disclosure body instead of the new handoff's full-height structured privacy disclosure sheet. The first compile pass also failed because `ModalBottomSheet` and `rememberModalBottomSheetState` require the Material3 experimental opt-in. |
| **Root Cause** | The sheet had not yet been migrated from the legacy `HandyGlassBottomSheet` overlay to the P-4 JSX `DisclosureSheet` structure, and the new Material3 API requirement was not declared on the composable during the first implementation pass. |
| **Fix** | Replaced `PrivacyDetailsBottomSheet` internals while preserving `fun PrivacyDetailsBottomSheet(onDismiss: () -> Unit, modifier: Modifier = Modifier)`: Material3 modal sheet with 0.55 scrim, 60dp top offset, custom drag handle, green shield-fill header tile, section eyebrows coded green/blue/red/amber, inline bold span for `your own API key`, custom red minus-glyph bullets for the won't-do list, and muted `SurfaceElevated` "I understand" CTA. Added the new `privacy_sheet_*` string ids while preserving `onboarding_disclosure_body` in resources. Added scoped `@OptIn(ExperimentalMaterial3Api::class)` so the build stays green. |
| **Validation** | Plain `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` is still blocked by the host's missing system Java runtime, so Gradle validation used the repo-local JDK. The first local-JDK assemble run caught the missing Material3 opt-in; after the fix, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed. `git diff --check` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, force-stopped and cleared app data, walked Splash -> Value, tapped "What Handy sees", confirmed the sheet opens with top bound `[0,180]` on the 3x-density emulator (60dp), confirmed title/sections/CTA via uiautomator, visually checked `/tmp/handy-privacy-sheet.png` against the JSX `08 · Privacy disclosure` artboard source, verified "I understand" returns to the Value screen, verified swipe-down dismissal returns to Value, and confirmed fresh logcat had no `AndroidRuntime` / `FATAL EXCEPTION` / Handy error output. |
| **Prevention Rule** | A "disclosure sheet" is not the same as a "tap-for-me confirmation sheet" — the disclosure CTA must remain muted so it doesn't compete visually with a primary action elsewhere on screen. When introducing Material3 modal APIs, declare the narrowest `@OptIn(ExperimentalMaterial3Api::class)` before the first compile lane. |

That layout did not match the new always-expanded hero-card handoff or the provider-group sheet intended to scale beyond Anthropic. The bottom-sheet code followed the existing privacy sheet dismissal pattern but initially missed one required Compose foundation import. |

Replaced the rendered Settings Brain block with the hero card and sheet state, keeping `state.settings?.claudeModelOverride == HandySettings.DEFAULT_CLAUDE_HAIKU_MODEL` as the active-model read and routing only `"sonnet-4-5"` / `"haiku-4-5"` through `onClaudeModelVariant(false/true)`. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched the app, reached Settings, verified the Brain hero card shows `AI Brain`, current Claude model, inline Anthropic key field, `Add an API key to connect`, and `0 req · today`; tapped `Change`, verified the near-full-height sheet, Anthropic/Google/OpenAI groups, disabled `Soon` cards for Opus/Gemini/GPT-5, and selected Haiku to confirm the sheet dismisses and the hero card updates. The Handy process stayed alive and fresh `AndroidRuntime:E ActivityTaskManager:E Handy:E` logcat output was empty. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When collapsing N radios into 1 card + 1 sheet, the sheet must still allow any future provider to slot in without ViewModel rewrite — keep the modelId → callback mapping in the SETTINGS layer, not in the sheet. |

### DL-095 — Brain picker audit corrected provider-line API ambiguity and sheet top-treatment fidelity.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #settings #brain-picker #visual-audit #rca` |
| **Severity** | Audit Fix / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/settings/SettingsActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/settings/sections/BrainSection.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/settings/sections/ModelPickerSheet.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | A post-compaction deep audit found no runtime breakage, but it did find two subtle fidelity risks in the new Brain implementation. First, the `BrainSection` public signature documents `providerLine` as provider text such as `Anthropic`, while the source JSX visually shows the composed line `Claude Sonnet 4.5 · Anthropic`; the first implementation passed the fully composed line from Settings, so a future caller following the public signature literally would render only `Anthropic`. Second, the picker sheet matched the explicit Material3 parameter list, but it did not yet carry over the JSX sheet's top border/elevation treatment. The preview also used the pre-composed happy path, so it did not exercise the public API shape. |
| **Root Cause** | The first pass over-weighted the prompt's explicit implementation bullets and accepted the live Settings caller as the only consumer, instead of reconciling the public composable contract, the example arguments, and the JSX visual line as separate invariants. Context compaction in the middle of the larger settings redesign made the acceptance pass too checklist-oriented: build, open sheet, select model, confirm no crash. That caught wiring and compile issues, but not the API-shape ambiguity or the missing non-functional sheet chrome from the handoff. |
| **Fix** | `BrainSection` now normalizes the display line internally: blank provider falls back to the selected model, an already composed provider line is preserved, and a provider-only value renders as `selectedModelLabel · providerLine`. `SettingsActivity` now passes `providerLine = "Anthropic"`, and the preview uses the same public API shape. `ModelPickerSheet` now shares a `sheetShape`, clips/fills the near-full-height content with the 24dp top corners, adds a subtle top stroke, and applies a dark sheet shadow to better match the JSX modal treatment while preserving the Material3 dismissal pattern. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the original JSX handoff regions (`scenes-settings.jsx` BrainCard and `scenes-sheets.jsx` model picker), the current Settings Brain block, `SettingsViewModel` signatures, `SettingsPrimitives`, and `PrivacyDetailsBottomSheet` sheet pattern. Confirmed the active model still reads from `state.settings?.claudeModelOverride == HandySettings.DEFAULT_CLAUDE_HAIKU_MODEL`, the existing `onClaudeModelVariant(Boolean)` and `onClaudeKeyChange(String)` callbacks are preserved, and the model-id to ViewModel mapping remains in `SettingsActivity`, not the sheet. `git diff --check` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed again on the current tree with `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`. Installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, launched Handy, opened Settings, verified the hero card hierarchy and bounds, opened the picker, verified the 60dp top offset, all provider groups, disabled `Soon` cards, selected Sonnet, confirmed the card updated to `Claude Sonnet 4.5 · Anthropic`, confirmed pid `5054` stayed alive, and confirmed fresh `AndroidRuntime:E ActivityTaskManager:E Handy:E` logcat output was empty. |
| **Prevention Rule** | For design-handoff ports, audit both public API examples and JSX literal layout. Previews should exercise the public API shape, not an already composed happy path. When explicit prompt bullets omit visual details that still exist in "layout per JSX", port the detail or record why it is intentionally omitted before accepting the pass. |

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #settings #capabilities #permissions #web-search #tutor-mode` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |

| **Symptom** | Settings still exposed capability-related controls as separate Modes, capability-disclosure, and Web Tools blocks, so Web search keys were always visible and permission-backed rows had no single read-only status surface. The new handoff calls for one cobalt Capabilities accordion with Screen reading, Voice input, Notifications, Web search, and Tutor mode. |
| **Root Cause** | The previous screen predated the accordion design and treated Tutor and Web search as normal persisted toggles in different sections. Permission status also was not read at the Settings entry point, which would have made any capability row drift from OS truth if implemented as a stored preference. |
| **Fix** | Added `CapabilitiesSection` with the requested public signature, cobalt `SectionCard`, `SectionHead`, three read-only permission rows, nested Web search key card, dashed top divider, and Tutor mode row. `SettingsActivity` now injects `AccessibilityStateMonitor`, refreshes accessibility/mic/notification-listener state on entry and `onResume`, registers an Activity-level `RequestPermission` launcher for `RECORD_AUDIO`, and opens Android Accessibility / Notification Listener settings from read-only rows. Web search and Tutor still route through the existing DataStore callbacks, and Brave/Jina/GitHub key callbacks are unchanged. `SwitchRow` now lets users tap the whole row, not only the tiny switch target. |
| **Validation** | Plain `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` is still blocked by the host missing a system Java runtime before Gradle starts. With the repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed, and `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed. `git diff --check` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554` (API 35), launched Handy, opened Settings through the chat header, confirmed the Capabilities card is expanded with all five rows, toggled Web search on and verified Brave/Jina/GitHub fields plus the `Brave verified · Jina + GitHub raise rate limits` footer appear, tapped Screen reading while accessibility was off and confirmed Android opened `com.android.settings/.Settings$AccessibilitySettingsActivity`, returned to Handy, and confirmed fresh logcat had no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `E/AndroidRuntime`, `E ActivityTaskManager`, or Handy error lines. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Permission-backed toggles must NEVER persist their own DataStore flag — the OS is the source of truth. |

Full chat prompt execution was not run because this emulator does not have a configured Claude key/accessibility action flow in this session; the policy-denial path is covered by `DefaultActionPolicyEngineTest`. |

### DL-098 — Automations audit removed stale legacy Settings helpers after accordion migration.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #settings #audit #rca #dead-code` |
| **Severity** | Audit Fix / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/settings/SettingsActivity.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | A post-compaction audit found `SettingsActivity` still contained private helper code for the old settings UI (`CapabilityPolicyRow`, `CapabilityPolicySection`, `SectionHeaderWithIcon`, `RevokedPackageList`, `ActionButtonCard`, and `ToggleCard`) after the rendered screen had moved to `BrainSection`, `CapabilitiesSection`, and `AutomationsSection`. The app compiled and the new Automations card rendered, but the stale helpers still referenced legacy capability/incognito/action concepts and made the migrated screen look more coupled than it actually was. |
| **Root Cause** | The migration removed the old render call sites first and relied on the compiler/build to catch bad references. Kotlin permits unused private declarations, so compile success did not expose the leftover helper block. Context compaction in the middle of the Settings redesign also made the first pass optimize for functional acceptance checks (storage, policy reasons, assemble, smoke) rather than a final dead-code grep over the entire edited file. |
| **Fix** | Removed the unreachable private helpers and their now-unused imports from `SettingsActivity`. Left the explicitly requested callback plumbing (`onNoActionsInIncognitoToggle`, `onReviewActionDisclosure`) intact so later Privacy/disclosure work can still wire those without reintroducing Activity-level API churn. No storage, ViewModel, policy-engine, or rendered Automations behavior changed. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the Automations JSX primitive definitions and the Kotlin `SettingsPrimitives` implementation for row/card sizes. Re-ran `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`, which passed. Installed the rebuilt debug APK on `emulator-5554`, opened Settings, scrolled to Automations, verified the full row set (`Tap-for-me`, `Type-for-me`, `Recipes`, `Triggers`, trigger pills, both stop rows, `Disabled apps`) and captured `/tmp/handy-automations-audit.png`. Bounds from the 480 dpi emulator confirm the 16dp horizontal card padding and row stacking match the shared JSX primitives at Android scale, and fresh logcat had no `E AndroidRuntime` / `FATAL EXCEPTION` output. |
| **Prevention Rule** | After replacing a rendered UI section with a new composable, run a symbol grep for every deleted helper/component name before accepting the migration. A green Kotlin build is not proof that private legacy UI code was removed. |

Fresh logcat had no `E/AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, or `E ActivityTaskManager` output. Physical Pixel 9 validation remains blocked because only `emulator-5554` is attached. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When a screen's information architecture changes fundamentally, every dead composable from the old IA must be deleted in the same PR — leaving them as "reference" creates drift. |

Some small labels also inherited default Compose line heights from the shared typography tokens, so mono counters, optional tags, pill tags, and disabled-app chip text occupied more vertical text box than the JSX `line-height: 1` specs implied. |

The Web Search status footer now carries the handoff's extra 2dp top offset without changing the surrounding row contract. |

| **Prevention Rule** | For shared Compose row primitives, port asymmetric padding and explicit line-height from the handoff literally. A visually small 2dp bottom-padding drift compounds at accordion card edges and is easiest to catch by expanding the lowest section and inspecting the terminal row against the rounded border. |

### DL-101 — Chat overlay RCA restored contextual app subtitle and removed out-of-scope quick question pills.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #overlay #chat-panel #rca #scope-control #visual-regression` |
| **Severity** | Regression Fix / Scope Boundary |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPanelContent.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayPresenter.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayPanelBridge.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPipeline.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayChatPanelService.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/foreground/HandyForegroundAppMonitor.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/overlay/OverlayPanelState.kt`, `core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/QuickPromptCatalog.kt`, `app/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayPresenterFsmTest.kt`, `core/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts/QuickPromptCatalogTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | Tapping the floating Handy widget in Google Photos opened the compact chat overlay with the generic subtitle `What would you like help with?` instead of the contextual `In Photos. What can I help you with?`, and it rendered two quick question pills (`Summarize this screen`, `Show me around`) that were not part of the current Settings/accordion scope and added unnecessary overlay complexity. Practically, this made Handy look less context-aware exactly at the moment where the product should prove it knows the current tool. |
| **Root Cause** | The recent Settings work did not directly edit the overlay, widget motion, navigation pointer, or agent-flight code. A scope audit with `git log 60fd40e..HEAD -- app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/widget app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/accessibility app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/agent app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/foreground core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/overlay core/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/core/prompts android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/agent android-runtime/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/runtime/action` returned only the Settings redesign commit, and `git diff --name-only 1a34da3..HEAD` showed the Settings-era tracked changes were Settings, settings persistence/policy, provider icons, tests, and the debug log. The visible overlay behavior came from older V2 overlay work: `QuickPromptCatalog` was introduced in `136106e`, expanded in `68c7005`, and wired for the "Help me read this" quick action in `f31c22a`. The panel header also depended on a generated greeting string rather than deterministically composing the foreground display label. When `refreshNow()` missed the underlying app during overlay focus changes, `panel.snapshot` was null and the header fell back to a generic message. Settings audits missed this because their acceptance path was bounded to Settings surfaces; no widget-tap overlay screenshot was included after the settings IA changes, and the old quick-prompt code was not flagged because it was not in the Settings diff. |
| **Fix** | Removed the quick question pill UI and the dead quick-prompt plumbing end to end: `PanelContent.quickPrompts`, `OverlayPanelCallbacks.onQuickPrompt`, `QuickPromptsRow`, `OverlayPanelBridge.submitQuickPrompt`, `quickPromptAction`, the quick-prompt to summarize-mode mapping, and the `QuickPromptCatalog` production/test files. `OverlayPresenter.onWidgetTap()` now always derives the subtitle through `panelGreetingFor(snapshot)`, producing `In <displayLabel>. What can I help you with?` when a real foreground tool exists and `What can I help you with?` only when no foreground tool is available. `PanelHeader` accents `toolContext.displayLabel` instead of only the app label so umbrella/site contexts can still be highlighted. `HandyForegroundAppMonitor` now caches the last non-Handy, non-IME, non-launcher foreground snapshot and clears it on launcher events or launcher-only refreshes, allowing a widget tap over Photos to survive transient accessibility-window misses without showing stale context on the home screen. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before: in Photos, Handy could open with `What would you like help with?` plus two suggestion pills, so the user had to infer whether Handy actually knew it was looking at Photos. After: in Photos, Handy opens with `In Photos. What can I help you with?`, keeps the input row as the primary action, and shows no quick pills. In real use, that means a user opening Handy over Google Photos, Maps, Gmail, or a shopping site sees the current tool named in the subtitle instead of a generic assistant prompt. |
| **Validation** | Symbol sweep passed: `rg -n "QuickPromptCatalog|quickPrompts|onQuickPrompt|submitQuickPrompt|quickPromptAction|QuickPromptsRow|Show me around|What would you like help with"` over app/core/runtime source and tests returned no hits. The only remaining `Summarize this screen` hit is a core orchestrator unit-test user message, not overlay UI. `git diff --check` passed. With the repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed. Installed the final debug APK on `emulator-5554`, enabled Handy Accessibility and overlay permission, opened Google Photos, tapped the floating widget, and captured `/tmp/handy-photos-overlay-final.png`: the panel showed `In Photos. What can I help you with?`, the `Photos` token was accent-colored, and no quick question pills were rendered. Fresh error-level logcat after the smoke had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `ActivityTaskManager:E`, or Handy error output; the expected debug lines showed `ForegroundAppMonitor.refreshNow: emitted Photos` and `OverlayPresenter: panel open, pkg=com.google.android.apps.photos label=Photos marks=18`. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Any user-visible IA or visual audit must include a scope audit plus at least one smoke screenshot for adjacent surfaces that can be reached from the changed flow. Settings-only work must still verify the floating widget overlay when Settings changes touch global settings, action policy, accessibility state, or debug-log acceptance around Handy's capabilities. Do not keep exploratory quick suggestions in the compact overlay unless they are explicitly scoped, screenshot-approved, and covered by a product-level acceptance test. |

Clean-installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, skipped onboarding into `ChatActivity`, confirmed the UI dump contained `Handy`, `Ready when you are`, `Summarize this screen`, `What's in this photo?`, `Set a 10-minute timer`, `Look this up online`, `Minimise`, and `Settings`, and captured `/tmp/handy-chat-v2-after-skip.png`. ### DL-103 — Composer migrated to a floating, translucent pill above the MessageList. Last bubbles dissolve via a 36 dp vertical gradient fade. IME-aware via imePadding().

| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #chat #composer #design-handoff` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/design/FloatingComposerV2.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The full-chat composer was still a bottom row inside the main column. Messages stopped above it, the last bubble did not slide underneath a floating control, and there was no gradient dissolve into the composer area from the chat handoff. |
| **Root Cause** | The earlier full-chat pass migrated the top bar and empty state first, leaving the composer inline with the scrolling layout. An inline composer owns vertical space instead of overlaying the chat surface, so the message list cannot create the prototype's underlay-and-fade behavior without a separate absolute-positioned layer and proportional list bottom padding. |
| **Fix** | Added `FloatingComposerV2` as a full-screen overlay layer with a 36 dp bottom fade, 16 dp side / 20 dp bottom pinning, 28 dp rounded translucent pill, 40 dp mic/send controls, `BasicTextField` input, listening transcript state, send clearing, and the existing `onSend`, `onVoiceStart`, and `onVoiceStop` callbacks. `ChatActivity` now renders its normal column inside a `Box`, overlays `FloatingComposerV2` in the same box, removes the old inline `ChatComposer`/`MicButton`/`SendButton`, and gives `MessageList` 140 dp bottom content padding. API 31+ true blur backdrop is deferred; v1 uses the specified translucent surface fill because blurring host content correctly requires a separate `RenderEffect` backdrop layer behind the pill. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before: after a long chat, the final answer sat above a fixed composer row, so the bottom of the screen felt like a separate footer. After: bubbles can scroll under the composer while the 36 dp fade makes the bottom content dissolve into the page. In everyday use, a user reading a Maps or Photos answer can keep scrolling naturally while the input pill floats ready at thumb height; focusing the input keeps the composer attached to the keyboard instead of leaving it stranded at the bottom. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the universal Cursor rules, `DEBUG_LOG.md` prevention rules, `scenes-chat.jsx` `ContextBar` pill and `FloatingComposer` regions, and the current `ChatActivity` composer/message-list regions. Symbol sweep confirmed `ChatComposer`, `MicButton`, and `SendButton` no longer exist in chat source and only the tool-row `ThinDivider()` remains. `git diff --check` passed. Plain `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` is still blocked before Gradle starts by this host's missing system Java runtime. With the repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed; `BasicTextField(decorationBox)` compiled, so the `OutlinedTextField` fallback was not needed. Installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, used onboarding's Skip path into `ChatActivity`, verified the UI dump contained the floating composer affordances (`Start voice`, `Ask Handy anything…`, disabled `Send`), focused the input and typed `hello`, verified the edit text held `hello` and `Send` became enabled, tapped Send, and confirmed fresh app logcat had no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, or Handy crash output. Physical Pixel 9 testing remains a manual follow-up because only `emulator-5554` is attached in this shell. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Any "floating" overlay on top of a scrolling list must (a) add proportional bottom padding to the list and (b) ship a gradient fade above it — otherwise the last item reads as cut off. |
