### DL-104 — Floating composer audit fixed adjacent chat chrome drift and recorded RCA for missed visual details.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #chat #audit #rca #design-handoff` |
| **Severity** | Audit Fix / Visual Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/design/FloatingComposerV2.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/design/ChatTopBarV2.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | A post-compaction audit of the floating-composer migration found the core composer requirements were present, but adjacent chat chrome still had subtle design drift: the top-bar action buttons were implemented as 40 dp circular controls instead of the handoff primitive's 32 dp / 8 dp-radius icon buttons, the animated live dot occupied an 18 dp layout box for a 6 dp visual dot, and the chat root still used the older background token while the new composer fade terminated in `HandyDesign.Colors.PageBg`. Those details could make the header spacing and bottom fade read slightly off even though the app built and the composer worked. |
| **Root Cause** | The misses were process misses, not platform blockers. The first composer pass optimized around the explicit `ChatComposer` replacement, build success, and a smoke test, while the worktree already contained untracked adjacent V2 chat files from earlier passes. Because untracked files do not appear in normal `git diff` output, the real changed surface was larger than the tracked diff suggested. Context compaction also made earlier top-bar and empty-state work feel "already accepted", so it was not revalidated line-by-line against `primitives.jsx`. Finally, UIAutomator reports merged/touch-target bounds for Compose nodes, so visual sizes like 32 dp icon buttons and 40 dp touch affordances can look larger in the XML unless paired with source inspection and screenshot review. |
| **Fix** | Re-audited the full chat handoff against the live Compose files. `ChatTopBarV2` now matches the JSX `IconButton` primitive with a 32 dp box, 8 dp radius, and 18 dp icon. `ChatLiveDot` now uses a 6 dp layout box, letting the animated ring draw optically without adding header width. `ChatActivity` now uses `HandyDesign.Colors.PageBg` as the root surface so the 36 dp composer fade dissolves into the exact page color. `FloatingComposerV2` was left intact after source audit: the callbacks, pending transcript, listening placeholder, disabled send state while listening/streaming, 40 dp mic/send discs, 28 dp pill, 8 dp internal padding, 16 dp side / 20 dp bottom pinning, 140 dp list padding, and `imePadding()` contract are present. True blur backdrop is deferred by design; the implementation intentionally keeps the translucent fill instead of adding a host-content `RenderEffect` layer. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before this audit fix, the user-facing composer already floated, but the surrounding header could still feel a little too roomy and the fade could meet a subtly different background token. After the audit fix, opening Chat shows the slimmer handoff header, a LIVE chip whose dot does not widen the title group, and a bottom gradient that blends into the same page background as the rest of the chat. In practical use, the final bubble can slide under the pill cleanly while the top chrome stays compact and consistent with the rest of the redesigned app. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the universal rules, `scenes-chat.jsx` (`FloatingComposer`, `ContextBar`), `primitives.jsx` (`IconButton`, `LiveDot`, `QuickPromptCard`), current `ChatActivity`, `FloatingComposerV2`, `ChatTopBarV2`, `ChatEmptyHeroV2`, design tokens, and the active git status. Grep confirmed no source definitions or calls remain for the old `ChatComposer`, `MicButton`, or `SendButton`; only the intentional `ThinDivider()` after `ToolNameBar` remains. `git diff --check` passed. An initial attempt to run assemble and test/lint in parallel caused a transient KSP generated-file `NoSuchFileException` because both Gradle invocations mutate `app/build`; rerunning serially passed. With the repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug --stacktrace` passed, and `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, skipped onboarding into Chat, verified empty-state prompt card bounds, fade/composer nodes, typed `audit`, observed `Send` enable, tapped send, saw the user bubble plus expected missing-API-key error with no crash, granted RECORD_AUDIO, tapped mic, verified `Listening…`, `Stop`, and disabled `Send`, tapped again to stop, and checked logcat for no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, Handy crash, or ANR output. The emulator reports IME show/hide via `ImeTracker`, but this image does not render a visible soft keyboard even with `show_ime_with_hard_keyboard=1`; source-level `adjustResize` + `imePadding()` is present, and final visual keyboard-rise confirmation remains a physical Pixel 9 manual step. |
| **Prevention Rule** | After context compaction or when a dirty worktree includes untracked UI files, audit the entire reachable visual surface, not only the named file. Use `git status --short`, source dimensions, handoff primitives, screenshots, and UI dumps together. Do not parallelize Android Gradle tasks that share the same build directory; run assemble/test/lint serially unless they are intentionally part of one Gradle invocation. |

### DL-105 — Chat active conversation redesigned to JSX `04b/04c · Active` + `04d · Reduced` spec. Hand-mark prefix, tool-use captions, context bar, day separator, reduced banner.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #chat #design-handoff #active-chat #reduced-mode` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/design/ChatBubblesV2.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | Full chat still rendered the legacy row stack for active conversations: user turns used the older accent bubble/avatar treatment, assistant turns used a framed chip bubble with avatar/timestamp, tool context lived in the old editable `ToolNameBar`, streaming used the old private dot implementation, and accessibility-off mode showed a plain nudge instead of the `04d · Reduced` experience. |
| **Root Cause** | The previous chat passes migrated the top bar, empty state, and floating composer first, leaving `MessageList`, `MessageRow`, `ToolNameBar`, and the accessibility nudge as older private helpers inside `ChatActivity`. That meant the existing state model already had the data needed for the redesign, but the active/reduced visual contract from `scenes-chat.jsx` had not been mapped onto every row variation. |
| **Fix** | Added `ChatBubblesV2.kt` with `UserBubbleV2`, `HandyBubbleV2`, `TapForMeCardInBubble`, `DaySeparatorV2`, `ThinkingDots`, `ContextBarFullV2`, `ContextBarPillV2`, `ReducedBannerV2`, and `ChatReducedHeroV2`. Rewrote `MessageList` to build the chronological row stream from persisted messages, pending user turns, streaming deltas, local overlays, and pending show-in-app actions; it now inserts five-minute day separators, renders user messages as right-aligned asymmetric SurfaceElevated bubbles, renders assistant messages with the bare hand prefix plus optional tool-use caption, keeps system messages as centered muted captions, shows thinking dots for empty and non-empty streams, and nests the existing show-in-app action inside a tap-for-me card that still calls the existing handoff callback. Replaced the old tool-name bar path with `ContextBarFullV2` under the top bar and replaced the old accessibility nudge with the reduced banner plus reduced empty hero. The context close affordance dismisses the current card locally and still calls `onSetToolName("")`; the ViewModel setter does not yet treat a blank label as a persistent disconnect path. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before: a user asking "What does Maps say?" saw older avatar bubbles, per-message timestamps under each bubble, and an editable tool-name strip that felt separate from the new composer. After: the chat reads like the handoff: `Today · 7:40 PM` appears as a centered pill, the user's request sits in a quiet right-aligned elevated bubble, Handy answers from a left hand-mark prefix with a muted tool-use line when tools were used, the show-in-app CTA becomes a compact `Tap for me` card, and accessibility-off users land on a banner plus the centered `I can still chat.` reduced-mode hero with safe Ask/Search prompts. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the universal Cursor rules, `DEBUG_LOG.md` prevention rules, `scenes-chat.jsx` lines 102-462, the requested `ChatActivity` sections, full `ChatViewModel` state around `ChatUiState`, `ChatEmptyHeroV2`, `ChatTopBarV2`, `FloatingComposerV2`, `HandyDesignTokens`, `HandyDesignType`, available drawable resources, and `ChatMessage`. Confirmed `ChatUiState` field names were kept unchanged and `message.searchToolsUsed` is still the source for tool-use captions. Grep confirmed old `ToolNameBar`, `AccessibilityNudgeBanner`, `AssistantAvatar`, `UserAvatar`, `StreamingDots`, `LoadingVerbChip`, and `MessageRow(` references are gone from chat source. `git diff --check` passed. Plain `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` is still blocked before Gradle starts by the host's missing system Java runtime. With the repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed, and `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554` (Android 15), launched ChatActivity through the launcher, verified the reduced banner, `I can still chat.` hero, Ask/Search cards, composer, then tapped `Ask me a question`; the UI rendered `Today · 7:40 PM`, a right-aligned user bubble, the expected no-API-key assistant/system failure rows, and logcat error filtering found no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, Handy crash, or ActivityTaskManager error output. Physical Pixel 9 active-context and real web-search/tool-use animation remain manual follow-up gates because this emulator has accessibility off and no Claude key. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When migrating MessageList rendering, every existing message-row variation (system, streaming, show-in-app card, pending-confirmation) must be re-tested in the new layout — don't assume they "just work" inside the new wrapper composables. |

### DL-106 — Active-chat audit corrected reduced-mode/context edge cases after compaction.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-24 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #chat #audit #rca #design-handoff #context-compaction` |
| **Severity** | Audit Fix / Visual Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/design/ChatBubblesV2.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The first active-chat migration built and rendered, but a line-by-line audit against `scenes-chat.jsx` found subtle drift: reduced mode could still render a stale context card if `currentToolName` was left from a prior detected app, the reduced banner used bottom padding instead of the JSX `4dp` top inset, tool-use captions could concatenate `read 1 screen · Maps · web searched` instead of matching the single caption pattern, long context app names had no ellipsis guard, and the unused pill variant made the whole chip clickable instead of only `Change`. |
| **Root Cause** | The migration happened after multiple chat passes and context compactions, so the implementation optimized for preserving behavior and passing build/smoke coverage before doing a pixel-level pass. The original prompt mixed two caption phrasings (`searchToolsLabel(...)` wiring and JSX/self-check `read 1 screen · Maps` copy), which made the first pass combine both rather than choosing the display pattern. Reduced mode was tested from a cold Handy/default context, so the stale-context case did not appear in the emulator smoke. The pill variant was created because the prompt required it, but it was not wired for v1, so its click-target exactness was missed during the functional route audit. |
| **Fix** | Gated `ContextBarFullV2` behind `accessibilityServiceEnabled` so reduced mode cannot show stale screen context, changed reduced-banner outer padding to `top = 4.dp` with no extra bottom inset, simplified the caption derivation to show `read 1 screen · <app>` when a real app context exists and fall back to `searchToolsLabel` only without an app label, added one-line ellipsis protection to full and pill context app labels, and moved the pill click handler from the whole chip to the `Change` text. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before the audit fix, a user who had been chatting about Maps and then disabled accessibility could see the reduced banner and an old Maps context card at the same time, which implied Handy could still see the screen. After the fix, reduced mode is honest and clean: banner plus reduced chat surface only. Similarly, a web-search-backed answer in a real app context now shows the concise handoff-style caption rather than a doubled-up tool phrase. |
| **Validation** | Re-read `scenes-chat.jsx` lines 102-462, `ChatActivity.kt`, `ChatBubblesV2.kt`, `ChatTopBarV2.kt`, `FloatingComposerV2.kt`, `ChatEmptyHeroV2.kt`, `HandyDesignTokens.kt`, `HandyDesignType.kt`, and `ChatViewModel.setToolName/currentToolName` paths. Confirmed `ChatUiState` and `ChatViewModel` fields remain unchanged; pending confirmation still renders as `ConfirmationDialog`; `ShowInAppCard` still calls the pending show-in-app handoff callback; `ContextBarPillV2` remains implemented but intentionally unused in v1. `git diff --check` passed. With the repo-local JDK, `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed and `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed the rebuilt APK on `emulator-5554` (Android 15), launched ChatActivity, verified via UI dump and screenshot `/tmp/handy-chat-audit-reduced.png` that reduced mode shows the top bar, 4dp-inset banner, no context card, centered reduced hero, composer, and after tapping `Ask me a question`, the day pill, right-aligned user bubble, Handy hand-prefix failure row, and system caption. Fresh error log filtering found no `AndroidRuntime`, `FATAL EXCEPTION`, Handy crash, or ActivityTaskManager error output. |
| **Prevention Rule** | After any context-compacted UI migration, run a second design audit that includes unwired-but-created variants, stale-state combinations, and exact outer padding. Do not let a cold-start smoke substitute for stateful transitions such as accessibility on → off or detected app → reduced mode. |

### DL-107 — Tool-name context bar replaced with sticky pill above the floating composer; Change opens inline-edit in the same pill shape.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-25 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #chat #context-pill #floating-composer #inline-edit` |
| **Severity** | Feature / Regression Risk |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/design/ChatBubblesV2.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | Active full chat still had two possible context surfaces: the full `ContextBarFullV2` card below the top bar and an unused pill variant intended for the floating-composer slot. The full card also carried a close affordance even though the current design expresses context changes through `Change`, not dismissal. |
| **Root Cause** | The active-chat migration landed the full card first and left the pill as an unwired variant. Once the floating composer gained `bottomChrome`, the canonical handoff shifted the context indicator down to the composer stack, but the old render path and its local dismissal state remained in `ChatActivity`. |
| **Fix** | `ContextBarPillV2` now owns read/edit state, renders the translucent `Chatting about <app> · Change` pill in read mode, and morphs in place into a focused `BasicTextField` with `Done` and `Cancel`. `Done` trims and commits only non-blank, changed names through `ChatViewModel.setToolName`; `Cancel` restores the current app label locally. `ChatActivity` no longer renders `ContextBarFullV2`, no longer keeps `dismissedContextName`, and passes the pill through `FloatingComposerV2.bottomChrome` whenever accessibility is enabled and `currentToolName` is a non-Handy label. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before: when Handy detected Settings, the user saw a context card near the top of chat while the composer floated separately at the bottom; dismissing that card created a local hidden state that did not actually disconnect the tool context. After: the single indicator says `Chatting about Settings · Change` directly above the composer and follows the composer above the keyboard. In real use, renaming `Settings` to `Play Store` updates the pill and chat memory path through the ViewModel; renaming it to `Handy` removes the pill because the normal `showContextBar` condition becomes false. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the universal Cursor rules, `DEBUG_LOG.md` prevention rules, the full requested `scenes-chat.jsx`, `ChatBubblesV2.kt`, `FloatingComposerV2.kt`, `ChatActivity.kt`, `ChatViewModel.kt`, and the legacy `ToolNameBar` implementation from `e1837fe~1`. Confirmed `FloatingComposerV2.bottomChrome` already has the required `(@Composable () -> Unit)?` signature. Grep confirmed `ContextBarFullV2` has no remaining source definition or render call, and `ContextBarPillV2` has exactly one app caller through `bottomChrome`. `git diff --check` passed. Plain `java -version` still fails on this host because no system JRE is installed, so validation used the repo-local JDK. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed, and `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched via the app icon, verified the full chat surface by UI dump (`Handy`, `Ready when you are`, prompt cards, composer, mic, settings, send), and fresh error-level logcat had no Handy, `AndroidRuntime`, or `FATAL EXCEPTION` entries. Physical Pixel 9 active-context inline-edit testing remains the required manual follow-up because only `emulator-5554` is attached here. |
| **Prevention Rule** | When two UI surfaces redundantly indicate the same state, such as a top context card and a composer-adjacent context pill, pick the canonical design surface and delete the old render path in the same PR. Any context indicator that is anchored to a floating composer must be wired through the composer slot so it inherits IME movement, fade spacing, and bottom chrome ownership. |

### DL-108 — LIVE chip in chat top bar now reflects AI brain readiness, not chat activity.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-25 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #chat #settings #api-key #brain-readiness` |
| **Severity** | Logic Bug / UX State Fix |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatActivity.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/chat/ChatViewModel.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The `LIVE` chip beside the Handy wordmark was wired to chat activity: existing messages, pending turns, streaming deltas, or voice state. That made an active chat without a Claude key look live, while an idle chat with a saved Anthropic key looked offline. |
| **Root Cause** | The top-bar indicator had drifted from its semantic source. `LIVE` means the AI brain is ready, so the canonical state is whether `KeyStore.get(KeyStore.KEY_ANTHROPIC)` returns a non-blank stored value, not whether the chat screen is busy. |
| **Fix** | Added `ChatUiState.brainReady`, `ChatViewModel.computeBrainReady()`, and `ChatViewModel.refreshBrainReady()`. The ViewModel computes readiness from `KeyStore.KEY_ANTHROPIC`, initializes it before collector launches, and `ChatActivity.onResume()` refreshes it after returning from Settings. `ChatScreen` now passes `state.brainReady` into `ChatTopBarV2` unchanged, so the chip's dot, label, color, animation, signature, and layout remain untouched. No KeyStore observer flow was added; if Settings later becomes an in-process dialog, extend KeyStore with a `keyChanges: SharedFlow<String>` and observe it from `ChatViewModel`. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before: a user could fresh-install Handy, send `hello` without any Claude key, and see chat activity drive the header toward a live-looking state even though the request would fail. A user who had already saved a key but was sitting idle on the empty chat could see no chip. After: a saved non-blank Anthropic key shows `Handy` plus the pulsing accent dot and `LIVE`, even on an idle empty chat; sending a message without a key shows the normal missing-key error and the chip stays hidden. Gemini, Brave, Jina, and GitHub keys do not light the chip. |
| **Validation** | Re-read the requested source files and confirmed `ChatTopBarV2` only renders the existing chip row when `live` is true, `ChatActivity` had no prior `onResume`, `ChatViewModel` already injects `private val keyStore: KeyStore`, `ChatUiState` is in-memory only, `KeyStore.KEY_ANTHROPIC` is `anthropic_api_key`, and `KeyStore.get()` returns null for blanks via `takeIf { it.isNotBlank() }`. `git diff --check` passed. With the repo-local JDK, `./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace` passed and `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed the debug APK on `emulator-5554`, cleared encrypted secrets, verified fresh chat had no `LIVE`, opened Settings and confirmed the AI Brain key field still had only the design-specified `Show key` and `Paste key` trailing actions, typed `testapikey`, committed it through the existing non-empty key path, returned to chat and saw `LIVE`, force-stopped and relaunched to confirm the persisted key still showed `LIVE`, cleared encrypted secrets for the no-key path, relaunched and sent `hello`, and verified the normal `No Claude API key. Add one in Settings.` error rendered while `LIVE` stayed hidden. Fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, app ANR, `E/AndroidRuntime`, Handy error, or ActivityTaskManager error output. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Semantic readiness indicators must read from the canonical persisted capability state that justifies the label. Activity-derived booleans are useful for spinners and progress, but they drift when reused for product semantics like "brain is ready." |

### DL-109 — Post-compaction audit removed Settings key-field UI drift from the LIVE-chip fix.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-25 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #settings #chat #design-handoff #post-compaction-audit` |
| **Severity** | Audit Fix / Scope Boundary |
| **File(s)** | `DEBUG_LOG.md`; transient pre-audit diff in `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/settings/sections/BrainSection.kt` was removed before final |
| **Symptom** | The first implementation of the LIVE-chip fix solved the readiness condition but also added `Save key` and `Clear saved key` icons inside the AI Brain key field during blocker exploration. That made the Settings field diverge from `scenes-settings.jsx`, which specifies a 48dp field with exactly two trailing icon buttons: eye and copy. The extra icons narrowed the text area, changed trailing affordance count from 2 to 3, and shifted alignment even though the original request explicitly said the LIVE chip visual should stay unchanged and did not ask for Settings UI changes. |
| **Root Cause** | The blocker investigation mixed two concerns: the desired chip truth source (`KeyStore.KEY_ANTHROPIC` has a committed non-blank value) and the Settings field's key-lifecycle UX. Because the emulator smoke made IME/focus-loss commit behavior feel brittle, the implementation over-corrected by adding explicit key-field controls instead of keeping the fix scoped and documenting the lifecycle follow-up. Context compaction made that temporary investigation path look like part of the intended final solution, and the first validation focused on functional key state transitions rather than re-reading the Settings handoff for exact icon count, width, and spacing. |
| **Fix** | Removed the transient Settings key-field UI changes and restored the existing `BrainApiKeyField` trailing affordances to the handoff shape: `Show key`/`Hide key` plus `Paste key`, each in the existing 32dp icon button slot. The final tracked product change is back to the intended chat path only: `ChatUiState.brainReady`, `ChatViewModel.computeBrainReady()`/`refreshBrainReady()`, and `ChatActivity.onResume()` plus `live = state.brainReady`. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before this audit correction, Settings could show extra save/clear icons that were not in the design. After the correction, Settings layout, sizing, spacing, and alignment return to the pre-existing design while the chat header still shows `LIVE` only when a committed non-blank Anthropic key exists. |
| **Validation** | Re-read `scenes-chat.jsx` lines 3-29, `scenes-settings.jsx` lines 263-275, `primitives.jsx` lines 204-240, `ChatTopBarV2.kt`, `ChatActivity.kt`, `ChatViewModel.kt`, `BrainSection.kt`, `SettingsViewModel.kt`, `KeyStore.kt`, and `ClaudeLlmClient.kt`. Confirmed the final source diff no longer touches `BrainSection.kt`, the top-bar composable itself is unchanged, `ChatUiState` remains in-memory only, no DI changes are needed, and no code path lights the chip from Gemini/Brave/Jina/GitHub. `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:test :app:test :app:lint --stacktrace`, and `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. On `emulator-5554`, UIAutomator confirmed no-key chat has no `LIVE`, Settings AI Brain field exposes only `Show key` and `Paste key`, non-empty Anthropic key commit changes Settings to `Connected & Ready`, returning to chat shows `LIVE`, relaunch with the saved key still shows `LIVE`, clearing encrypted secrets and sending `hello` without a key shows the standard missing-key error and no `LIVE`. Fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, app ANR, `E/AndroidRuntime`, Handy error, or ActivityTaskManager error output. |
| **Prevention Rule** | After a context-compacted blocker investigation, explicitly separate "diagnostic workaround" from "final scoped fix" before final validation. For design-sensitive surfaces, validate not only behavior but also the handoff contract's affordance count, dimensions, spacing, and unchanged files. If a blocker suggests changing an adjacent UI, either make that a separate scoped task or document it as follow-up instead of silently folding it into the current patch. |

### DL-110 — Floating widget square halo removed while preserving thinking, flight, and pointer behavior.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-05-26 |
| **Tags** | `#android #compose #overlay #floating-widget #visual-regression #rca` |
| **Severity** | Bug Fix / Visual Regression |
| **File(s)** | `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/widget/design/WidgetGlyphV2.kt`, reviewed `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/FloatingWidgetOverlayService.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/BuddyFlightDriver.kt`, `app/src/main/kotlin/com/handy/app/widget/BezierFlightController.kt`, `app/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/BuddyFlightLandingGeometryTest.kt`, `app/src/test/kotlin/com/handy/app/overlay/OverlayPresenterFsmTest.kt`, `DEBUG_LOG.md` |
| **Symptom** | The floating Handy widget visually read as a square-ish overlay with a soft rectangular haze behind the orange circular hand disc. On light hosts the haze appeared as a pale square around the 48dp circle; on dark hosts it appeared as a visible dark/amber rectangular block. This made the widget feel like a square floating element rather than a pure circular control. The likely blast radius included non-idle states because thinking, web-search/streaming, flying, pointing, and acting all render through the same `WidgetGlyphV2` root. |
| **Root Cause** | The active overlay service no longer uses the older `WidgetContent` lens path for the draggable widget. `FloatingWidgetOverlayService.attachOverlay()` renders `WidgetGlyphV2`, whose root is intentionally a 64dp transparent canvas so touch handling, pointer pulse headroom, and the flying trail have room around the canonical 48dp disc. Inside that canvas, `WidgetGlow(glowColor)` was drawn before the disc for every state. On Android 12+ it blurred a 48dp colored circle with `Modifier.blur(radius = 18.dp, edgeTreatment = BlurredEdgeTreatment.Unbounded)` and then applied `background(color, CircleShape)`; on older APIs it stacked 60/54/48dp soft circles. The unbounded blur and fallback soft circles were circular in intent, but they were rasterized inside the rectangular 64dp Compose layer/window. Because the overlay window uses `PixelFormat.TRANSLUCENT` and `WRAP_CONTENT`, the semi-transparent pixels at the layer boundary made the whole canvas footprint visible as a square halo behind the circle. State-specific colors made the same bug possible in both amber idle/thinking/listening/acting states and blue flying/pointing states. |
| **Fix** | Removed only the ambient `WidgetGlow` layer and its `glowColor` selection from `WidgetGlyphV2`. The 64dp root canvas remains unchanged for hit testing and animation headroom. The 48dp `WidgetDiscSize` circular fill/border remains clipped to `CircleShape`. `DiscGlyph`, `ListeningBars`, `ThinkingArc`, `FlyingTrail`, pointer hand rotation, pointer scale, flying wobble, and acting badge remain intact. No `WindowManager.LayoutParams`, touch/drag/long-press handling, presenter FSM mapping, flight controller math, landing geometry, alpha fade, or pointer-pose updates were changed. |
| **Behavior Change** | Before: Handy could show a soft square around the circular disc in idle and likely in thinking/web-search/streaming/flying/pointing because every state shared the same glow layer. After: the floating widget visual is just the circular disc plus the intentional state chrome: listening bars inside the disc, the thinking arc around the disc, the blue flying trail behind pointer movement, the pointer hand orientation, and the acting corner badge. The widget still occupies the same touch canvas, so drag, tap, long-press, flight landing, and sticky pointing behavior are unchanged. |
| **Validation** | Source audit confirmed `FloatingWidgetOverlayService` renders `WidgetGlyphV2` as the active draggable overlay; `BuddyState.THINKING`, `STREAMING`, and `PREPARING_POINT` still map to `WidgetState.THINKING`, `BuddyState.FLYING` maps to `WidgetState.FLYING`, `BuddyState.POINTING` maps to `WidgetState.POINTING`, and `BuddyState.ACTING` maps to `WidgetState.ACTING`. `BuddyFlightDriver` still drives only `moveBuddyTo`, `updatePointerPose`, `updateBuddyAlpha`, and presenter state transitions during flight. `BezierFlightController` still owns the Bezier path, reduced-motion fade, dwell pulse, and cancellation callbacks. Plain `./gradlew :app:compileDebugKotlin` was initially blocked by the host's missing system Java runtime. Using the Gradle-provisioned repo-local JDK, `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17-aarch64-os_x.2/jdk-17.0.18+8/Contents/Home ./gradlew :app:compileDebugKotlin` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.gradle/jdks/eclipse_adoptium-17-aarch64-os_x.2/jdk-17.0.18+8/Contents/Home ./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests com.handy.app.overlay.BuddyFlightLandingGeometryTest --tests com.handy.app.overlay.OverlayPresenterFsmTest` passed. `git diff --check` passed. Existing untracked `handy_macos_ref/` was left untouched. |
| **Prevention Rule** | Do not add blurred or semi-transparent ambient layers to the floating widget root canvas unless the layer is clipped or masked to the same circular silhouette that the user should perceive. The widget may keep a larger transparent touch/animation canvas, but every visible decorative pixel outside the 48dp disc must be deliberate state chrome with a bounded shape, such as the thinking arc or flying trail. When changing widget visuals, audit all states that share the composable (`IDLE`, `LISTENING`, `THINKING`, `FLYING`, `POINTING`, `ACTING`) and run flight/FSM tests so visual cleanup does not mutate pointer behavior. |

---

## DL-FW-1 — WidgetGlyphV2 replaces legacy WidgetContent in the floating overlay service
- All glyphs migrated to HandyDesign tokens. Layers A (colored glow) -> B (disc fill + border) -> C (per-state glyph). Outer canvas stays 64dp to preserve the existing pulse band.
- AccentGlow / PointGlow / PointTrail added to HandyDesign.Colors.
- ACTING is now a distinct widget state with a bolt corner badge (Surface fill, 1.5dp Accent border, ic_bolt at 10dp). Service mapping updated: BuddyState.ACTING -> WidgetState.ACTING.
- Flying renders the same pointer geometry as Pointing (no arrow) and adds a 36x16 dp trail BEHIND the disc, anchored 22 dp left of the disc centre, rotated by `pointerRotationRadians.toDegrees()` around the disc center so it always trails opposite to motion. Trail alpha is driven by pointerScale > 1.02f with a 120 ms fade so the trail disappears cleanly on Flying -> Pointing.
- Pointer drawable swapped from ic_pointer_hand to ic_phosphor_hand_pointing_bold. Both vectors default to finger-up; the existing `+ 90f` rotation offset is preserved.
- Pre-API-31 blur fallback uses three layered soft circles (10/18/30 % alpha) instead of Modifier.blur.
- WidgetContent.kt is retained — UnifiedBuddyContent, WidgetBubbleChip, ManualTargetFallbackChip still consume the legacy composable. They will be migrated when we touch the bubble system.

## DL-OV-1 — OverlayQuickChatPanelV2
- Panel chrome rebuilt to the polished quick-chat spec: 28 dp top-only corners, HandyDesign.Colors.PanelSurface near-opaque fill, 1 dp AccentHairline top outline, 42x4 dp drag handle, subtle amber glow above the sheet, and 20/18/20/28 dp internal padding so it sits flush over gesture nav.
- Quick-chat backdrop blur uses the bounded pre-panel screenshot path, clipped to the sheet and drawn through PanelBackdrop. WindowManager FLAG_BLUR_BEHIND / blurBehindRadius are intentionally not used on the full-screen overlay host because they blur the whole display behind that host. If screenshot capture is unavailable, the panel uses an opaque PanelSurface fallback.
- Input row migrated to the larger 60 dp composer with 44 dp symmetric mic/send controls, 16 sp text, and host placeholders limited to Maps / Photos / default. Listening row, streaming row, confirmation chip, error chip, response preview, and bubble footer remain on HandyDesign tokens.
- Quick prompt chips were removed from the minimized overlay panel; the composer is the only input affordance in this surface.
- PanelGreetingCategory + categorizer moved to a shared internal file so the panel can read the category without depending on the presenter.
- R.drawable.ic_phosphor_mic and R.drawable.ic_phosphor_send were present. HandyDesign.Colors.PanelSurface was added for the near-opaque quick-chat panel fill.

## DL-OV-2 — Overlay panel blur and amber border correction
- Root cause: the quick-chat host is a full-screen MATCH_PARENT TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY so `FLAG_BLUR_BEHIND` / `blurBehindRadius` blurred the whole foreground app. Android's API docs also distinguish blur-behind from background blur: blur-behind affects the screen behind the window, while background blur is bounded to a Window. Handy's raw WindowManager overlay view does not have a panel-sized Window, so window-level blur was the wrong primitive here.
- Fix: removed all runtime use of `FLAG_BLUR_BEHIND`, `blurBehindRadius`, `FLAG_DIM_BEHIND`, and the cross-window blur flag path from OverlayChatPanelService. Restored the bounded accessibility-screenshot pipeline, passed the snapshot through OverlayChatPanelContent into OverlayQuickChatPanelV2, and reused PanelBackdrop/BlurredBackdropSnapshot clipped inside the rounded sheet. Screenshot conversion now runs on a single background executor, and the capture timeout is 240 ms so the panel blur resolves reliably without reintroducing main-thread StrictMode slow-call warnings.
- Glow/hairline fix: replaced the rectangular glow strip and straight drawLine hairline with `topSheetOutlinePath(...)`, which traces the same top-left arc -> top edge -> top-right arc as the 28 dp top-only sheet shape. The soft amber glow is drawn as wide translucent strokes behind that path, and the 1 dp AccentHairline is drawn over content on the same rounded path, leaving the side and bottom edges unbordered.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug`, `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest`, and `./gradlew :app:lintDebug` passed after the final timeout/executor adjustment. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554` and opened the panel over Home, Google Play Store sign-in, and Maps sign-in/onboarding. Visual screenshots confirmed the host UI remains sharp outside the sheet, the panel sits flush to the bottom edge, and the amber halo follows the rounded top border rather than appearing as a detached straight stripe. `dumpsys window` for the panel window showed only `LAYOUT_IN_SCREEN LAYOUT_NO_LIMITS HARDWARE_ACCELERATED` flags, with no blur or dim flags. Fresh logcat scans found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no post-fix StrictMode warning from screenshot conversion. A non-fatal system `W/AppOps: Noting op not finished` warning appeared during screenshot/overlay interaction on the emulator; it did not crash the app and did not correspond to an AndroidRuntime error.

## DL-OV-3 — Overlay panel halo replaces hard amber top line
- Root cause: the previous amber treatment still rendered as edge geometry: the panel drew a stroked top path over its content and a wide stroked path behind it. On bright hosts this could read as a chopped-off amber line, and at the 28 dp corner transition the stroke shape exposed a visible kink.
- Fix: removed all panel top-edge stroke drawing from OverlayQuickChatPanelV2. Added `PanelHalo`, a separate 24 dp-tall Box stacked immediately above the panel in the same animated Column so it travels with slide-in/slide-out. The halo draws only a vertical gradient (`0.00 -> 0 alpha`, `0.55 -> 0.05`, `0.85 -> 0.14`, `1.00 -> 0.22`) clipped to a custom Path whose only rounded corners are the bottom corners, mirroring the panel's 28 dp top corners. The panel container itself now has no top border or amber draw pass.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug`, `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest`, and `./gradlew :app:lintDebug` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, opened the overlay over the white Google Play Store sign-in screen and a darker home/Maps-context screen, and captured screenshots. The white-host screenshot shows a soft amber bloom fading upward into transparent with no hard 1 px stroke above the rim. The darker-host screenshot keeps the halo subtle. Fresh logcat filtering for `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `AndroidRuntime`, `ANR`, `StrictMode`, `Path`, `clip`, and `render` found no halo-related runtime errors or rendering warnings. The existing non-fatal `W/AppOps: Noting op not finished` emulator warning still appears during screenshot/overlay interaction and remains unrelated to this halo drawing path.

## DL-OV-4 — Overlay halo corner path no longer folds upward
- Root cause: `PanelHalo` was 24 dp tall but its custom bottom-rounded path used the panel's 28 dp corner radius without clamping. On high-density screens this made the arc rectangles extend above the halo bounds, visually folding the glow so the strongest amber appeared to sit on the wrong side of the halo on dark backgrounds.
- Fix: clamp the halo path radius to `min(requestedRadius, haloHeight, width / 2)` before building the bottom-corner arcs. The gradient stops are unchanged; only the clip silhouette is corrected so the alpha still increases toward the panel edge without the path inverting.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug`, `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest`, and `./gradlew :app:lintDebug` passed. Installed the APK on `emulator-5554`, opened the overlay on a dark home background matching the reported failure mode, and captured `/tmp/handy-halo-radius-clamped.png`. Fresh logcat filtering for `FATAL EXCEPTION`, `AndroidRuntime`, `ANR`, `StrictMode`, `Path`, `clip`, `render`, `OverlayChatPanelService`, and `OverlayPresenter` found no halo-related runtime errors or rendering warnings.

## DL-OV-5 — Overlay halo removed in favor of a thin sheet outline
- Root cause: after testing the halo against dark hosts, the gradient treatment still added too much ambiguity above the minimized chat sheet. The desired next iteration is simpler: no bloom/gradient and only a thin Handy-themed outline on the overlay sheet itself.
- Fix: removed the overlay halo/render path entirely from `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2`: no `PanelHalo`, no `topSheetOutlinePath`, no `drawBehind`/`drawWithContent` amber glow, and no gradient/path imports remain in the panel file. Added a single `0.5.dp` `HandyDesign.Colors.AccentHairline` border to the rounded sheet container after `clip(sheetShape)`, so the theme outline follows the whole panel shape while the panel remains flush to the bottom edge.
- Scope audit: the internal chat content was intentionally left unchanged. The diff touches only imports, the sheet chrome modifier, and removal of the old halo/path helper; `DragHandleV2`, `PanelHeaderV2`, `ContextLineV2`, `InputRowV2`, listening/streaming/error/confirmation rows, response preview, bubble footer, state branching, and callbacks are unchanged by this pass.
- Validation: `git diff --check` passed. Grep confirmed no `PanelHalo`, `buildPanelHaloPath`, `Brush.verticalGradient`, `drawBehind`, `drawWithContent`, `topSheetOutlinePath`, `drawLine`, or `drawPath` remain in `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2`. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554` and launched `com.handy.android`; the app resumed to `ChatActivity` with no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no Handy/overlay exception in logcat. The fresh error log contained unrelated emulator/system noise from Play Store (`Finsky`) and `TaskPersister`; debug-level StrictMode disk-read/write messages also appeared in existing startup paths (`AccessibilityStateMonitor`, `JsonHistoryStore`) and were not introduced by this overlay chrome change.

## DL-OV-6 — Overlay panel outside taps dismiss again
- Root cause: the quick-chat polish moved the panel host to a full-screen `MATCH_PARENT` overlay for correct IME resizing, then intentionally changed the dismissal model to "close button owns dismissal." `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2` only had an empty sheet `clickable` to swallow taps inside the panel; the transparent area around the sheet had no scrim click handler. Since the full-screen overlay window still intercepted touch, outside taps neither reached the foreground app/widget nor called `OverlayPanelCallbacks.onDismiss`. If the floating widget ever did receive a second tap while the presenter was already in `ChatPanel`, `FloatingWidgetOverlayService` still routed the normal tap to `presenter.onWidgetTap(...)`, which refreshes/reopens the panel instead of closing it.
- Fix: restored the scrim-dismiss pattern in `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2` by adding a full-size transparent `Box` behind the bottom sheet with `onClick = callbacks.onDismiss`; the sheet keeps its no-op clickable so panel content and empty sheet space do not dismiss accidentally. Updated the service comment to describe the transparent scrim contract. Added a defensive widget toggle path in `FloatingWidgetOverlayService`: if `ACTION_DOWN` starts while `OverlayMode.ChatPanel` is active, the gesture arms `panelDismissTapArmed`, skips long-press voice start, ignores drag movement, and on `ACTION_UP` calls `panelBridge.cancelVoiceFromPanel()` plus `presenter.dismissPanel()`. `ACTION_CANCEL` just clears the armed state.
- Scope audit: no composer/header/body UI internals were changed. The panel-content diff adds only `scrimInteractionSource` and the transparent scrim layer; the existing sheet body, outline, backdrop blur, input row, state branching, and all child composables remain unchanged.
- Validation: `git diff --check` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:assembleDebug :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, confirmed the floating Handy widget was present on Home, tapped the widget to open the overlay panel, verified the panel window appeared as a full-screen application overlay plus the widget overlay, tapped outside the sheet and verified the panel overlay window was removed while the widget remained. Reopened the panel and tapped the widget's screen location; the panel overlay was again removed, proving the second-tap close path works through the full-screen scrim. Fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no Handy overlay exceptions. Remaining error-level logs were unrelated emulator/system or Play Store (`Finsky`) noise.

## DL-OV-7 — panelGreetingFor catalog expanded
- Added expanded category coverage for PHOTOS, MUSIC, VIDEO, MESSAGING, SOCIAL, CALENDAR, NOTES, BANKING, FOOD, RIDE, and FILES. Each non-banking category picks a one-line greeting that contains the tool label verbatim so `greetingWithLabelAccent`'s case-insensitive `.indexOf()` match still highlights the label in Accent.
- BANKING intentionally returns a label-less generic string so Handy doesn't appear to be "in" a banking app. This keeps the surface neutral on PII-sensitive screens.
- Categoriser ordering tightened: Photos -> Camera -> Browser -> Maps -> Settings -> Email -> Messaging -> Social -> Video -> Music -> Calendar -> Notes -> Banking -> Food -> Ride -> Phone -> Files -> Shopping -> Default. This ordering is needed because some social/video apps share substrings; the broad YouTube video matcher excludes YouTube Music so it can still land in MUSIC.
- Test coverage added for each expanded category, every label-accentable greeting, and the banking no-label exception.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:assembleDebug`, and `./gradlew :app:lintDebug` passed with the repo-local JDK. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.android` to Onboarding, and fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no `Process com.handy.android has died` entry.

## DL-OV-8 — Groww, Spotify, and Netflix get app-specific panel greetings
- Added app-specific `panelGreetingFor` overrides for Groww, Spotify, and Netflix before the broader category copy runs. The strings stay short, include the display label verbatim for Accent matching, and avoid changing panel chrome.
- Groww package detection now tags `groww` under the finance-sensitive category so the catalog recognises the app family even though the app-specific override uses a label-accented stock-market line.
- Test coverage added for exact Groww, Spotify, and Netflix copy plus Groww category recognition.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:assembleDebug`, and `./gradlew :app:lintDebug` passed with the repo-local JDK. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.android`, and fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no Handy process-death entry.

## DL-OV-9 — Quick-chat V2 now renders presenter-owned panel greetings
- Root cause: `OverlayPresenter.onWidgetTap()` correctly wrote the category-specific string into `PanelContent.greeting`, but `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2.ContextLineV2` ignored that field and rebuilt its own `In <app>. What can I help you with?` string from the package-manager app label. Unit tests covered presenter state only, so the visible Compose panel regressed while the catalog tests stayed green.
- Fix: `ContextLineV2` now renders `panel.greeting` directly and applies a small `greetingWithLabelAccent()` helper to accent `toolContext.displayLabel` only when the literal label appears in that greeting. Removed the duplicate context-line string builder so the presenter remains the single source of greeting copy.
- Test coverage: added a unit test for the accent helper preserving full greeting text and a Compose instrumentation test that renders `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2` with the Netflix-specific greeting and asserts the old generic Netflix line is absent.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:assembleDebug`, `./gradlew :app:lintDebug`, and targeted `./gradlew :app:connectedDebugAndroidTest -Pandroid.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments.class=com.handy.app.overlay.OverlayQuickChatPanelV2Test` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.android`, and fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no Handy process-death entry.
- Prevention rule: whenever presenter state feeds a Compose surface, include at least one renderer-level test for the displayed text, not only a presenter/unit test for the backing state.

## DL-OV-10 — RCA: greeting catalog regression came from a split source of truth
- Timeline: commit `0f0c77a` reintroduced contextual overlay subtitles through `OverlayPresenter.panelGreetingFor()` and stored the chosen copy in `PanelContent.greeting`. Commit `35c48fc` then rebuilt the quick-chat panel as `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2`, moved category lookup to shared code, and added a new `ContextLineV2` renderer that independently formatted `In <currentAppDisplayName>. What can I help you with?` from `PackageManager`. That made the visible panel stop consuming `PanelContent.greeting` even though the presenter still populated it. Later commits `72206df` and `270278c` expanded the presenter greeting catalog and added unit tests, but those tests asserted presenter/catalog output only; they never rendered `OverlayQuickChatPanelV2`, so the app kept showing the V2 renderer's hardcoded generic line.
- Why it looked like it "worked earlier": before the V2 rebuild, the visible overlay subtitle path and the presenter-owned `PanelContent.greeting` path were effectively the same path. After the V2 rebuild, the panel still showed a context-looking line with the correct app label, so smoke tests that only checked "does it say Netflix/Groww/Photos?" could pass by eye while the category-specific second sentence was lost. The label accent also masked the issue because `ContextLineV2` still highlighted the app name, making the UI look plausibly connected to the catalog.
- Why it "went away" after the latest commits: the latest fix removed the renderer's duplicate sentence builder and forced `ContextLineV2` to render `panel.greeting` directly. `greetingWithLabelAccent()` now only decorates the label inside the existing greeting; it does not choose or rewrite copy. The presenter is again the single source of truth for greeting text, and the V2 panel is a pure renderer for that text.
- Why our process missed it: the OV-2/OV-8 tests were below the UI boundary. They proved `panelGreetingFor(snapshot)` returned correct strings and `OverlayPresenter.state.value.panel.greeting` held them, but they did not assert the actual Compose tree displayed those strings. This is the classic "state is right, renderer ignores state" failure mode.
- Permanent prevention rule: for every user-visible string stored in presenter/view-model state and rendered by a Compose surface, add one renderer-level test that mounts the actual composable and asserts the visible text. If a renderer needs to style part of a state-owned string, expose a styling helper that preserves `AnnotatedString.text == sourceString`; never let the renderer reconstruct product copy from adjacent fields like app label/package name. Grep checks for old fallback copy are useful but insufficient unless paired with a rendered-node assertion.

## DL-VOICE-1 - Voice replies now speak through Android system TTS
- Root cause: `ConversationOrchestrator` already emitted `AssistantTurnFinalized.ttsText`, but both overlay quick chat and full chat ignored it. `AndroidTtsClient` was bound in runtime DI and chunked speech correctly, yet no app-layer owner called it or mapped its lifecycle back to the Buddy widget.
- Fix: added core `SpeechAudioState` (`IDLE`, `PREPARING`, `SPEAKING`, `STOPPING`, `ERROR`) and appended `BuddyState.AUDIO_SPEAKING` without changing existing enum ordering. `OverlayPanelState.audioState` now tracks audio independently from visible response bubbles. Added `SpeechOutputController`, which speaks only nonblank `ttsText` for voice-triggered turns, respects `HandySettings.speakVoiceRepliesAloud` (default true), de-dupes consumed request IDs to prevent overlay-to-chat double-speak, cancels pending start jobs on barge-in/tap/dismiss, and reports state to `OverlayPresenter.onSpeechAudio(...)`. Overlay and full-chat pipelines now call the controller only from voice finalized events; typed turns and streamed deltas never call TTS.
- Stop behavior: `VoiceController.start()` stops speech with `new_listen` before STT begins. Overlay errors stop with `turn_error`. Panel dismiss, action handoff dismiss, widget tap, and widget drag stop with explicit diagnostic reasons. The widget maps `AUDIO_SPEAKING` to the existing listening waveform glyph until a dedicated audio glyph lands later.
- Settings/UI/docs: DataStore persists `speak_voice_replies_aloud` with a safe default of true. Brain settings now show "Speak voice replies aloud" under the TTS provider controls. README current state now says voice replies are spoken aloud via Android system TTS and notes Sarvam output as P-VOICE-2.
- Test coverage: added `SpeechOutputControllerTest` for null/blank ignored, disabled setting, new-request stop-before-speak, synchronous stop state, and pending-start cancellation. Added overlay pipeline, full chat voice, and voice-controller tests for the voice/typed/error matrix and barge-in ordering. Added presenter FSM coverage for audio glyph state, core settings JSON coverage, and Android TTS queue-flush coverage for `speak(A); speak(B)`.
- Validation: `git diff --check` passed. `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity`, and fresh error log filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no Handy process-death entry. Manual physical Pixel audio validation was not run in this local session; the remaining release gate is the Pixel checklist for actual speaker output, toggle behavior, long-press barge-in, tap-to-stop, drag-to-stop, and panel-dismiss stop latency.
- Prevention rule: every future voice-output path must consume `ttsText` only, be keyed by orchestration request ID, and keep audio state orthogonal to response-bubble text. Any new dismissal or listening entry point must call `SpeechOutputController.stop(reason)` before it changes UI or starts STT work.

## DL-VOICE-2 - Deep audit hardening after context compaction
- Audit scope: re-read the original voice-output requirements against every changed production path: core speech/overlay models, `DataStoreSettings`, runtime TTS, `SpeechOutputController`, overlay quick-chat pipeline, full chat ViewModel, `VoiceController`, floating widget service, overlay panel service, action-session panel dismissal, settings UI, README, and the new unit coverage. Grep checks confirmed the only production `TtsClient.speak(...)` caller is `SpeechOutputController`, neither streamed deltas nor `event.chatText` are spoken, all direct production `presenter.dismissPanel()` call sites stop speech first, and `BezierFlightController`, `ConversationOrchestrator`, and `AssistantMarkupParser` were not touched.
- Finding fixed: `AndroidTtsClient.isSpeaking` was read by `SpeechOutputController` from app coroutines and written by Android TTS callbacks, but the flag was not volatile. It is now volatile, and the active/final utterance IDs are tracked so callback-thread updates are visible to the controller.
- Finding fixed: chunked TTS could set `isSpeaking=false` after an intermediate chunk's `onDone`, even though queued chunks could still play. `AndroidTtsClient` now keeps speaking true until the final chunk completes and clears on active utterance errors/stops/release. `TtsChunkerTest` now locks this with a chunked lifecycle test in addition to the queue-flush barge-in test.
- Finding fixed: widget tap/drag stop was originally wired on drag transition and tap `ACTION_UP`. That met the nominal call-site requirement but not the stricter "within 100ms" tap-to-stop UX when a user held the pointer down, dragged while the panel was armed for dismiss, or touched a sticky pointer while audio was protected from the buddy glyph. `FloatingWidgetOverlayService` now stops non-idle audio on `ACTION_DOWN`, while preserving the existing `buddy_tap`/`buddy_drag` later calls as harmless diagnostic reinforcement.
- Finding fixed: `SpeechOutputController` had the core happy-path tests but did not explicitly prove same-request de-dupe or "SPEAKING only after TTS reports playback." Added tests for same request ID speaking once, `PREPARING -> SPEAKING -> IDLE` driven by fake `isSpeaking`, and pending-start cancellation before settings resolve. The controller start/stop critical section is synchronized so a tap/listen stop cannot race between settings read completion and `tts.speak(...)`.
- Finding fixed: the first full-suite rerun exposed a test isolation bug in `ChatViewModelVoiceTest`: `Dispatchers.Main` was reset before all `viewModelScope` cancellation work drained. Each test now cancels the ViewModel scope and advances the scheduler before reset, so the voice tests can run inside the full `:app:testDebugUnitTest` suite without leaking coroutine failures into the next test.
- Why the misses were missed: the initial implementation was split by context compaction, and the final review leaned on targeted tests plus `git diff --stat`, which undercounted untracked new test/source files. That caught first-order wiring, DI, and build failures but did not stress callback-thread visibility, multi-chunk TTS lifecycle, physical touch timing (`ACTION_DOWN` vs `ACTION_UP`), or whole-suite test isolation. The audit changed the checklist from "does the requested call exist" to "can any asynchronous edge start, continue, or report speech after the user tried to stop it."
- Validation: targeted `:android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.speech.TtsChunkerTest'` and `:app:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.app.voice.SpeechOutputControllerTest'` passed after the fixes. `git diff --check` passed. Full `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed the rebuilt `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity`, and fresh error log filtering found no Handy `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, and no process-death entry. Physical Pixel speaker-output timing remains the required manual release gate.
- Prevention rule: voice-service changes require both path-level tests (overlay/full chat/voice controller) and lifecycle tests for the underlying TTS adapter. Any "stop within 100ms" UX must be wired at gesture start (`ACTION_DOWN` or equivalent), not only at click completion. Whole-suite test runs must follow targeted runs before logging acceptance.

## DL-UI-ICON-1 - Brain settings tile now uses the supplied Lucide brain SVG
- Symptom: after rebuilding, the Settings `AI Brain` card still showed the old custom brain outline instead of the supplied Lucide brain icon.
- Root cause: the Brain card wiring was already correct (`BrainSection` uses `SectionTile(R.drawable.ic_brain, SectionTone.AmberBrain)`), and no alternate `drawable`/`drawable-*` brain resource existed. The miss was that `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_brain.xml` itself still contained the old hand-authored vector path. In the prior pass, the dirty/context-compacted worktree was treated as if the resource replacement had landed, but reopening the actual source file showed it had not persisted in the current tree.
- Fix: replaced only `app/src/main/res/drawable/ic_brain.xml` with the path data from `/Users/satvik.bansal/Downloads/brain.svg`, preserving the existing 24 dp vector resource contract and letting `SectionTile` continue to apply theme tint. No Kotlin settings code, model settings, or other drawables were changed for this fix.
- Validation: `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :app:mergeDebugResources --rerun-tasks --stacktrace`, `./gradlew :app:mergeReleaseResources --rerun-tasks --stacktrace`, and `./gradlew :app:packageReleaseResources --rerun-tasks --stacktrace` passed. Grep confirmed source, debug packaged resources, release merged resources, and release packaged resources now contain the Lucide path (`M12 18V5`) and no old custom path (`M9,4.5` / `strokeWidth="1.4"`).
- Prevention rule: for drawable-only replacements, always re-open the final resource file after patching and force a resource task with `--rerun-tasks` before claiming the app will display the new asset.

## DL-VOICE-3 - Sarvam Bulbul TTS provider
- Root cause: Settings already exposed `TtsProvider.SARVAM` and Sarvam voice choices, but runtime DI still bound `TtsClient` directly to `AndroidTtsClient`. The settings controls were therefore aspirational: selecting Sarvam could not route speech to Sarvam, store a Sarvam key, choose a spoken language, test the voice, or fall back through a production switcher.
- Current Sarvam contract check: Sarvam's May 2026 docs list `POST https://api.sarvam.ai/text-to-speech` with request fields `text`, `target_language_code`, `speaker`, `model`, and `output_audio_codec`; `bulbul:v3` is the current model. The implementation follows that current contract rather than the older `inputs` / `audio_format` body shape.
- Fix: added `SarvamLanguage` (`AUTO`, `ENGLISH`, `HINDI`, `HINGLISH`) and persisted `sarvam_spoken_language` in DataStore. Added `EncryptedKeyStore.KEY_SARVAM` / `KeyStore.KEY_SARVAM`. Added `AudioPlayback` plus `MediaPlayerAudioPlayback`, which writes each generated WAV chunk under `cacheDir/tts`, plays it with `MediaPlayer`, and deletes tracked files immediately on completion/stop/release with a 5s deferred cleanup fallback.
- Runtime routing: added `SarvamTtsClient` with cancellable OkHttp calls, 8s connect / 15s read timeouts, current Sarvam REST JSON, `MAX_CHUNK=2400`, reuse of `AndroidTtsClient.chunkOnSentenceBoundary`, serial chunk synthesis/playback, 401/403 key clearing, 429/5xx/network fallback to System TTS, and no API-key logging. Added `SwitchingTtsClient` so `TtsClient` now routes System vs Sarvam from `HandySettings.ttsProvider` and key presence; exceptions in the active provider fall back to System TTS.
- Privacy/audit: Sarvam audit rows store provider, model, language code, character count, latency, Sarvam request ID when present, and success/fail/cancel result. They do not store the spoken text, generated audio, or API key.
- Settings/UI: Brain settings now show a Voice provider picker. When Sarvam is selected, the card reveals voice, spoken-language, Sarvam API key, and Test voice rows. The test button calls the injected production `TtsClient`, so it exercises the same switcher used by real voice replies. Missing Sarvam key is shown as an "Add key" hint and runtime falls back to System TTS.
- Tests: added `SarvamTtsClientHttpTest` for happy path base64 decoding, 401 key-clear fallback, 429 fallback, 5xx fallback, >2400 character ordered chunk requests/playback, and in-flight cancellation with no playback. Added `SwitchingTtsClientTest` for settings-flow routing, no-key fallback, and active-provider exception fallback. Extended `TtsChunkerTest` to lock Sarvam's reuse of the Android sentence chunker and `HandySettingsJsonTest` to cover the new language default/round trip.
- Validation: `git diff --check`, `./gradlew :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.speech.SarvamTtsClientHttpTest'`, `./gradlew :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest`, and `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest` pass with the repo-local JDK. Full `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew :core:test :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed after lint/test output and debug APK packaging completed successfully.

## DL-VOICE-4 - Sarvam TTS deep audit hardening
- Audit scope: re-read the original Sarvam TTS prompt against the generated code and its dependencies: `HandySettings`, DataStore persistence, encrypted key storage, runtime DI, `AndroidTtsClient` chunking, Sarvam HTTP synthesis, playback/cache cleanup, switcher routing, settings UI, the `SpeechOutputController` voice gate, audit logging, global log redaction, README, and all new tests. Grep confirmed normal voice replies still call TTS only through `SpeechOutputController.speakForVoiceTurn(...)` using orchestrator `ttsText`; typed responses and streamed chat text are still not spoken. The Settings "Test voice" button is the only direct production `TtsClient.speak(...)` caller, by design, and it routes through the production switcher.
- Findings fixed: `SwitchingTtsClient.speak(...)` now stops the inactive provider before starting the selected provider, so a System utterance cannot overlap a Sarvam test or vice versa. Blank stored Sarvam keys no longer count as configured. `MediaPlayerAudioPlayback` now sweeps stale `cacheDir/tts/*.wav` files on construction, stop, and release in addition to per-playback tracked deletion, covering process-restart residue; it also uses a playback generation guard so files written after a racing stop/release are deleted immediately. `SarvamTtsClient` now uses a generation token so a cancelled older synthesis job cannot clear `isSpeaking` for a newer utterance, and all `speak(...)` calls cancel any in-flight Sarvam job before missing-key fallback. Sarvam failure audit rows now include `fallback=system`, and the app-wide log redactor explicitly handles Sarvam's `api-subscription-key` header.
- Test coverage added in the audit: Sarvam HTTP tests now assert selected voice/language request fields, missing-key direct fallback without HTTP, network-down fallback with fallback audit, success audit privacy (no spoken text in `semanticTarget`), and fallback audit marking. Switching tests now prove the inactive provider is stopped before the selected provider speaks. Crash diagnostics tests now cover Sarvam's subscription-key header redaction.
- Why the misses were missed: the first implementation spanned context compactions and focused on the core route/fallback contract, so tests mocked playback and did not exercise provider handoff overlap, stale process cache files, or stale coroutine state after cancellation. `git diff --stat` also hid untracked new files, making the change feel smaller than it was during the first review. The settings test path was easy to miss because normal voice turns correctly pass through `SpeechOutputController.stop(...)`, while the Test Voice button intentionally calls `TtsClient` directly. The KSP cache incident during this audit was caused by running two separate Gradle invocations concurrently against the same generated stores; it was resolved by stopping the daemon, clearing generated KSP output, and rerunning validation serially.
- Validation: targeted speech tests passed with `./gradlew --no-daemon :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.speech.SwitchingTtsClientTest' --tests 'com.handy.runtime.speech.SarvamTtsClientHttpTest' --stacktrace`; targeted Sarvam HTTP tests passed after adding the network-down case; targeted privacy test `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.app.privacy.CrashDiagnosticsFormatterTest' --stacktrace` passed. Final full validation also passed: `git diff --check && JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew --no-daemon :core:test :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.android`, and a fresh logcat scan found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `E/AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, no forced Handy finish, and no Handy process-death entry.
- Prevention rule: voice-provider work must test both the provider implementation and the switcher boundary. Any direct `TtsClient.speak(...)` production caller must be listed explicitly and must either call `stop()` first or rely on a switcher that stops inactive providers. Generated audio storage needs a startup/release sweep, not only per-file happy-path deletion. Do not run separate Gradle/KSP builds concurrently in this repo; use one full command or serial invocations to avoid generated-cache corruption.

## DL-VOICE-5 - Voice settings audit tightened status semantics, accessibility, and switcher state
- Audit scope: re-read the Sarvam Bulbul TTS prompt and the later Voice-section redesign prompt against the current dirty tree after context compaction. The pass covered `HandySettings` defaults and DataStore keys, encrypted Sarvam key storage, Sarvam HTTP schema, runtime DI, `AndroidTtsClient` chunker reuse, `SarvamTtsClient`, `SwitchingTtsClient`, `MediaPlayerAudioPlayback`, settings state mapping, the new honey Voice accordion, shared settings primitives, README/current-state, and the voice-related tests. Sarvam's current public docs were rechecked and still use `POST /text-to-speech` with `text`, `target_language_code`, `speaker`, `model`, and `output_audio_codec`; the implementation follows that current schema rather than the older `inputs`/`audio_format` prompt draft.
- Finding fixed: the Voice footer used `Connected & Ready` for System TTS because the first UI prompt originally said "System provider, master ON" should be green connected. Product clarification made that copy misleading: "Connected & Ready" must mean a key-backed provider is configured. Voice now has a separate `SystemVoice` status that renders `Using System voice`; `Connected & Ready` is only emitted for Sarvam with a nonblank saved key. The emulator was switched System -> Sarvam to prove System hides Sarvam-only rows and renders `Using System voice`, while Sarvam with a saved key still renders `Connected & Ready`.
- Finding fixed: `SwitchingTtsClient.isSpeaking` only checked the currently selected provider. If settings changed while the previous provider was still speaking, Settings could show the wrong Test Voice state and a Stop action could look unavailable. It now reports `system.isSpeaking || sarvam.isSpeaking`, and a regression test covers speech continuing across a provider switch.
- Finding fixed: the shared pill selectable semantics exposed only the pill label/tag, not the row context, so TalkBack could announce "Sarvam" without "Voice provider". `PillSelectRow` now passes the group title into each pill's content description, producing descriptions such as `Voice provider · Sarvam`, `Sarvam voice · Ritu`, and `Spoken language · Auto` while keeping the existing radio-button role.
- Finding fixed: the Sarvam key masking helper could expose too much of very short entered values by always appending the final four characters. It now shows only a prefix plus dots for short secrets and keeps the prefix/dots/suffix pattern for normal API-key lengths, with the existing middle ellipsis for long keys.
- Why the misses were missed: the first Voice-section implementation mixed a product-state requirement from the UI prompt ("System provider is connected") with the later wording requirement ("Connected & Ready only when an API key is entered"). Because both compiled and the Sarvam-with-key path looked correct in emulator, the copy bug survived until the explicit clarification. The `isSpeaking` gap was a cross-provider state bug: routing tests asserted which provider received `speak(...)`, but not what happens when user-visible playback state outlives a settings switch. The pill semantics gap was a nonvisual accessibility issue; screenshot/device smoke can confirm layout, but not whether each selectable carries enough spoken context. The short-key mask gap was a privacy edge case hidden by normal long API-key fixtures.
- Validation: `git diff --check` passed. Targeted validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew --no-daemon :app:compileDebugKotlin :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.speech.SwitchingTtsClientTest' --stacktrace`. Full validation passed with `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew --no-daemon :core:test :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace`; the only notable compiler warning was the pre-existing deprecated `LocalClipboardManager` API usage. Installed the rebuilt APK on `emulator-5554`, opened Settings, verified the Voice card sits below AI Brain with honey tone, Sarvam selected with a saved key shows voice/language/key/test rows and `Connected & Ready`, switching to System shows `Replies spoken · System voice`, removes Sarvam-only rows, and shows `Using System voice`. Screenshot proof for the System footer was captured at `/tmp/handy-settings-voice-system-footer.png`. A search for settings screenshot tests found no settings-specific screenshot suite in the repo; only diagnostics screenshot coverage exists, so emulator UIAutomator/screenshot smoke covered this visual regression request locally.
- Prevention rule: every settings status label must have a single semantic owner and test/check language that distinguishes "built-in system path is usable" from "external key-backed provider is configured." For voice providers, switcher tests must assert both routing and exposed `isSpeaking` state across provider changes. Accessibility audits must inspect semantics content, not just visual screenshots. Secret-mask helpers need fixtures for short, normal, and very long values so privacy behavior is explicit.

## DL-STT-1 - Android STT mode, Hindi/Hinglish language routing, and low-confidence confirmation
- Root cause: `AndroidSttClient` had one implicit English/on-device-first policy (`en-US`, `EXTRA_MAX_RESULTS=1`) and only emitted a flat final transcript. That made Hindi/Hinglish behavior invisible to tests, made the privacy/network trade-off impossible to choose, and left low-confidence Android recognizer results free to auto-submit into the LLM even when alternatives were available.
- Fix: added orthogonal STT settings (`SttMode` and `SttLanguage`) alongside the existing provider enum. Runtime DI now snapshots DataStore settings into an `AndroidSttConfig` per listen session: Auto keeps DL-015 on-device language-pack fallback and DL-024 forced-online retry, On-device only fails closed without network fallback, and Network allowed skips on-device recognition. `AndroidSttClient` now requests `EXTRA_MAX_RESULTS=3`, threads the selected language tag, enables formatting on API 33+, and enables Android 14 language switching for Hinglish with `en-IN`/`hi-IN` where supported.
- Result model: `SttEvent.Final` now carries alternatives, first-result confidence, and whether the final came from an on-device recognizer while preserving default values for existing transcript-only call sites. Result parsing keeps the top three nonblank recognizer strings and treats unavailable confidence scores as null.
- UI/control flow: low-confidence finals (`confidence < 0.55`) with alternatives no longer auto-submit. `VoiceController` asks `OverlayPresenter` to show a confirmation chip in the quick panel and returns no transcript to the overlay/full-chat submit path until the user chooses a heard phrase or types over it. Settings now shows STT mode and recognition-language pickers plus Hinglish Android 14+ code-mix copy.
- Test coverage: added Robolectric `AndroidSttClientTest` coverage for on-device-only fail-closed behavior, no retry on on-device pack-missing errors, preserved AUTO DL-015 fallback, language-tag threading, API-gated Hinglish language-switch extras, and top-three/confidence parsing. Added `VoiceControllerTest` coverage for low-confidence confirmation vs high-confidence direct submission and extended settings JSON round-trip defaults.
- Validation: `git diff --check` passed. Targeted `./gradlew --no-daemon :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.runtime.speech.AndroidSttClientTest' :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests 'com.handy.app.voice.VoiceControllerTest' --stacktrace` passed. Full `JAVA_HOME=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17 PATH=/Users/satvik.bansal/.cache/codex-jdk17/bin:$PATH ./gradlew --no-daemon :core:test :android-runtime:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug --stacktrace` passed. Installed `app-debug.apk` on `emulator-5554`, launched `com.handy.app.onboarding.OnboardingActivity`, and fresh logcat filtering found no `FATAL EXCEPTION`, no `AndroidRuntime`, no ANR, no force-finish, and no Handy process-death entry; `pidof com.handy.android` returned a live process.
- Prevention rule: STT changes must keep provider selection separate from runtime mode selection. Any future recognizer provider must expose alternatives/confidence through `SttEvent.Final` and must prove low-confidence alternatives cannot reach chat submission without an explicit user pick.

## DL-STT-2 - Deep audit hardening after STT context compaction
- Audit scope: re-read the original STT prompt against the current diff after compaction, covering settings serialization, DataStore persistence, runtime DI, `AndroidSttClient` mode/error state machine, Android SDK-gated extras, result parsing, `VoiceController`, overlay presenter/bridge state, settings UI rows/strings, diagnostics display, and affected voice/chat tests. Grep confirmed no new cloud STT provider was added, no audio persistence was introduced, and existing transcript-only call sites still read `Final.transcript`.
